### Post #149: 2024-08-18 19:40

**For anyone that’s just getting started**

This journey comes with a lot of pain and gain always make sure you learn from a loss. Chart is 👑💯. Use other tools like data and flow to add base to your thesis. Learn how to use moving averages and volume to identify fake outs. Learn to identify support and resistance for entry and exit. And most importantly memorize as many formations and patterns as you can preferably the ones that validate the most. This will help you atleast have a clue as to what the market is trying to do. All these things take time. Don’t get overwhelmed by opportunities don’t hold contracts for 10 different companies focus on what you can you’ll gain more. Don’t let fear make you sell at a loss. And always put your emotions in a bottle and throw that shit away. OH AND ALWAYS BE HUMBLE YOU CAN BE A G AT THIS ONE DAY AND GET DUMPED THE NEXT. FOCUS ON THE BIGGER PICTURE. 


For swing traders- I suggest the monthly, daily, weekly, 30 min and 15 min chart

For weekly trades I suggest the 15 minute 5 minute and the minute chart (keep in mind fake outs occur a lot in these time frames always use higher time frames for direct confirmation)

Last but not least. Have fun 🥂

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/g2V/for-anyone-thats-just-getting-*

### Post #172: 2024-08-20 20:07 — `$SPY`

**Final thoughts $SPY**

I started mid day when I saw the beginning of a bear flag forming I drew out the possible path it would be taking and adjusted as needed as more candles appeared eventually the downs and ups got filled favoring the formation.

So far 2 of the triangles have been filled and in the AfterHour $SPY is already working its way back up to fill the 3rd

### Post #501: 2025-01-02 19:01 `[Discuss]`

**Quick lesson**

Identifying lower highs / lower lows to point out a downtrend

And using it to identify reversals 


<No lines no wedges no moving averages no volume>

{train your eyes excercise} 

For basic understanding

If candle wicks are longer at the bottom selling pressure is being bought up

If candle wicks are longer at the top buying pressure is being sold  to identify support (bottom) resistance (top) you usually follow along with the area that most candle wicks bounce off of.

And to confirm the end of a consolidation period. The highest point must break and become support 🎯💯

Enjoy. Any questions feel free to ask

(Quick GPT breakdown of the importance of these levels)

1.	38.2% Level: Indicates a shallow retracement, often seen in strong trends. Buyers may step in early, signaling bullish momentum remains intact.
	2.	50% Level: While not a true Fibonacci number, it’s widely used as a psychological level. It suggests a moderate retracement where buyers may look to re-enter, maintaining the trend’s structure.
	3.	61.8% Level: Known as the “golden ratio,” it represents a deeper retracement. If the uptrend holds at this level, it suggests strong underlying support and a potential for the trend to resume.

### Post #515: 2025-01-07 18:51 `[Chart]`

**Trading with EMA (5/20/50/200)**

5 EMA. Micro term trend used to track strength of trend (resistance strong bearish trend strong support strong bullish trend strong)

20 EMA short term trend

The price trades above the short term trend 
EMA trend bullish continuation expected In The short run


200 EMA long term trend

If price trades above long term trend bullish continuation in the long term



If price trades below short term trend line it is a short term down trend ( risky plays if not below 200 EMA) 

(Make sure the price hasn’t broken out below the long term trend line this means it’s not a real downtrend)


Strong trend = aggressive entry

Watch line slopes if lines are wide out from the price = trend is strong

If line slopes are going through the candles = the trend is weak (wait for entry)

When smaller 20 EMA is above larger 50 EMA 

PRICE IS IN UPTREND (calls opportunity 🐂)📈

when smaller  (💚) 20 EMA is below mid term (💛)50 EMA

PRICE IS IN DOWNTREND  (puts opportunity 🐻) 📉 (even better if below 200 day aswell)

(Higher time frames hourly 30 +)

When candle sticks break ⛓️‍💥 through (💚) 20 EMA from top ⬆️ to bottom ⬇️ = uptrend 📈🚫over downtrend📉✅ begins
( vice versa)

In an uptrend buy when candle sticks test support and bounce (EMA) 

5 EMA micro term = blue stepped line 🩵
20 EMA short trend = Green 💚
50 EMA mid term trend = yellow 💛
200 EMA long term trend = red ❤️


Disclaimer always pair indicators up with other things like price action volume etc indicators alone aren’t always reliable do your own research and trade smart 🥂❤️

Next part Fibonacci Retracement

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/wrj/trading-with-ema-52050200*

	5.	Bearish Projection:

**Ultimate $SPY analysis**

Time frames: 4hr - Daily 

In a market full of uncertainties it is always important to prepare for both possibilities. We are completely aware that the market does two things. It’s goes Up or down. Now to dive deep and mark out levels to trigger the strength in which the market will lean towards next thats a different story. This brings me to our first image two outcomes with confirmations. Spy is coiling up in what is known as a symmetrical bullish wedge as price fluctuates it nears its time to make a move. It has been clear that bulls currently have the upper hand if they confirm a break out of this wedge and invalidate the bearish formation (double top on the daily chart) we can see shorts being covered and a rally to $615 🎯. All else fails and we get unexpected news that causes the market to reject any bullish momentum (downtrend target $588) below I marked out levels of confirmation to watch for great put entries (with take profit levels that align with possible reversal zones {FVG zone + 61.8 Golden Fibonacci level}) The daily chart shows a bullish flag with a break and retest inside of the symmetrical triangle. This is yet another bullish confluence. Currently most MA’s are starting to lean bullish (additional confluence) what you want to see tomorrow is a possible 20 day MA retest on the daily (601 area) that holds! Or full control of bulls through bullish momentum this will allow bulls to drive the price back up to challenge all time highs And hopefully proceed victoriously above the 611 zone 💡⬆️

### Post #716: 2025-02-28 04:57 — `$SPY` `[Chart]`

**$SPY**

Part 1 

🚨 SPY Analysis & Market Outlook 🚨

📉 Bearish Signals Dominating the Charts 📉

The market structure on SPY is showing strong bearish indications, and based on previous patterns, we could see further downside. Let’s see what’s going on here.

1️⃣ Double Top Formation – A classic reversal pattern signaling exhaustion of bullish momentum. Price action confirms this as we see a rejection from the highs.

2️⃣ Bearish Falling Wedge Breakout – A breakdown from this pattern suggests continued selling pressure. Watch for a possible retest of the breakdown area around 590 – if this level holds as resistance, expect further downside.

3️⃣ Moving Average Cross – The 20-day MA bearishly crossing the 50-day MA historically led to a drop from 539 to 510. If the same play unfolds, we could be looking at SPY testing 565-550 before finding support.

4️⃣ RSI Bearish Divergence – Momentum is fading, aligning with the price drop. The RSI is also confirming this bearish outlook.

what does this mean? : If 590 acts as resistance, a continued drop toward the 565- 550 zone is likely.


Second image 

1️⃣ Bearish Engulfing on the Daily – Strong confirmation of seller control. The first engulfing candle set the stage, and now we see an even larger bearish engulfing candle, indicating increased selling volume.

2️⃣ 5-Day MA Lost Momentum – The short-term moving average has turned bearish, reinforcing the downtrend.

3️⃣ Target Levels – Based on previous support zones and volume levels, key areas to watch are 568-565, with a potential deeper correction toward 550.

### Post #757: 2025-03-10 02:43 — `$SPY` `[Chart]`

**Ultimate $SPY Breakdown Pt. 3**

$SPY has been in a clear downtrend, forming a falling wedge with a strong support base at the July highs and the 200-day MA (~569-570). There are some signs of potential bottoming, but confirmation is needed for a bullish reversal.

Key Levels & Targets:
	•	Support:
	•	569-570 – 200-day MA & July Highs (Crucial for holding structure)
	•	565.23 – If broken, downside extends to 560-550 range
	•	Resistance:
	•	574-578 – Short-term resistance within the wedge
	•	578-580 – 5 MA, 20 MA, and 50 MA cluster
	•	587-590 – Major resistance zone, needs volume to clear

Bullish Confirmation:
	•	A breakout above 578-580 with volume and sustained support on MA levels would signal the first sign of strength.
	•	Falling wedge breakout confirmation can trigger a short squeeze toward 590-595 if momentum builds.

🚀 Bullish Case (If SPY Reclaims 565 & 574-575)
	•	565 is the pivot – reclaiming this level opens the door for a push higher.
	•	A breakout above 574-575 confirms a bullish falling wedge breakout, setting up for higher prices.
	•	Targeting 598-600, where sentiment could shift back to bullish.

✅ 565 is the battle zone – SPY needs to reclaim it for bulls to have control.
✅ 555 is the last major support before risk increases toward 505.
✅ Bulls need a strong move above 574-575 to confirm a trend reversal.

For now, SPY is leaning bearish, but a reclaim of key levels could flip momentum. Stay patient and trade the levels! 📊

Incase you’re wondering what a Bear/Bull territory means here’s a quick breakdown!

Bear vs. Bull Territory – What It Means for Price

🔻 Bear Territory (Below 565-555): Sellers control the market, pushing prices lower with lower highs & lower lows. Support levels (555, 550, 533, 510, 505) become key—if they break, downside accelerates. High selling volume confirms bearish momentum.

🚀 Bull Territory (Above 574-600): Buyers regain control, leading to higher highs & higher lows. Old resistance turns into support, and above 600, sentiment fully flips bullish. Strong buying volume confirms momentum.

📌 The 565-574 zone is the battle area—price decides whether to break down or push higher.

**Addressing the Elephant in the room**

Some people might wonder why I don’t charge more or why I charge at all. The simple answer is I want this to be accessible. Trading education shouldn’t be locked behind a paywall so high that only a few can afford it. $10 is a price that allows me to provide value while filtering out people who aren’t serious. It helps me keep this community running while ensuring that those who join are invested in actually learning, not just looking for a quick handout.

Why I Focus on Building Traders, Not Just Giving Signals

If all you want is buy/sell alerts without understanding why you’re taking a trade, this probably isn’t the right place for you. I’m here to help you become self-sufficient. My goal isn’t just to make you money on a single trade—it’s to give you the skills to make money consistently, with or without me. The best traders aren’t the ones who rely on signals; they’re the ones who understand why they’re entering and exiting trades. If you don’t know that, you’re just gambling.

### Post #814: 2025-03-31 04:10

**Dear Long-Term Investors,**


The markets are going through a rough period, and it’s understandable if you’re feeling uncertain. Whether you’re holding long-term positions, averaging down, or wondering if you made a mistake by “buying the dip,” here’s how to navigate this environment.

The Dip That Keeps Dipping 🩸🩸

If you’ve been buying the dip but the market keeps heading lower, you’re not alone. Many investors misjudge where the true bottom is, especially in bear trends. A few things to consider:

• Are you averaging down smartly? Instead of going all in, consider scaling in gradually at key support levels
• Look at the bigger picture. If you’re holding fundamentally strong assets, volatility is part of the long game
• Have a cash buffer. It’s okay to sit on cash while waiting for confirmation before deploying capital

Understanding Market Cycles 🔁 

Markets move in cycles, and this downturn isn’t the first or last we’ll see. Historically:

• Corrections and bear markets are natural and set the stage for the next bull run
• Patience is key. Long-term investors who stay the course typically outperform those who panic-sell at the lows
• Don’t fight the trend. If markets are in a clear downtrend, sometimes the best move is to wait for strength before committing more capital

If You’re Holding Heavy Bags 💰🔥

If you’re deep in red on positions, now is the time to reassess:

• Is it a temporary drawdown, or is your investment thesis broken
• Do you have a plan for risk management? Setting a max loss or stop-loss level can prevent further damage
• Consider hedging. Some investors use put options or inverse ETFs to offset losses

Long-Term Mindset Wins 🥂

• Zoom out. If you believe in the market long-term, downturns are opportunities, not disasters
• Invest in quality. Strong companies and sectors will recover faster than speculative plays
• Stay adaptable. The best investors adjust their strategies when conditions change


🏆🤞

This is a tough time, but tough times create the best investors. Stay patient, stay informed, and remember every bear market in history has eventually led to new highs.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/Jjg/dear-long-term-investors*

### Post #817: 2025-03-31 20:50 — `$SPY`

**$SPY**

Example of using Supply and Demand to make a trade 

Supply and demand zones represent areas on a chart where buying or selling pressure has been strong, leading to price reversals or consolidations. Understanding these zones and their breakouts helps traders make informed decisions about entries, exits, and stop-loss placements.

Supply and Demand Consolidation Zones
	•	Supply Zone (Resistance): A price area where selling pressure exceeds buying, causing the price to stall or reverse downward.
	•	Demand Zone (Support): A price area where buying pressure exceeds selling, causing the price to stall or reverse upward.
	•	Consolidation Zones: These occur when price moves sideways within a defined range, signaling indecision between buyers and sellers.

Breakouts from Consolidation
	•	Bullish Breakout (Demand Zone Holds or Resistance Breaks)
	•	Price breaks above resistance, indicating strong buying momentum.
	•	Often followed by a retest of the breakout level before continuing higher.
	•	Volume confirmation adds validity to the breakout.
	•	Trading Decision: Enter long on breakout confirmation or retest, set stop-loss below support.



	•	Bearish Breakout (Supply Zone Holds or Support Breaks)
	•	Price breaks below support, signaling strong selling momentum.
	•	Can be followed by a retest of the broken support (now resistance).
	•	Volume confirmation strengthens breakout reliability.
	•	Trading Decision: Enter short after confirmation, set stop-loss above resistance.

### Post #828: 2025-04-04 11:47

**The Tale of Timmy the Trader 🚶🏻‍♂️**

Timmy the trader, fresh and so bold,
Dreamed of great riches, of silver and gold.
Armed with his phone and a brand-new app,
He thought he’d get rich in a virtual snap.

One fateful morning, he spotted a dip,
“A bargain!” he cheered, with excitement to grip.
The chart was red, the price went down,
But Timmy just grinned no sign of a frown.

He jumped right in, he bought the plunge,
Ignoring the pros who warned, “Don’t lunge!”
“A falling knife?” He laughed, “Not me!
I’ll catch it fast and ride it free!”

But as he clicked, the stock fell lower,
His dreams of wealth began to sour.
His hands got cut, his wallet bled,
His hopes of Lambo dreams went dead.

He watched in horror, full of regret,
His margin called a crushing bet.
And as he stared at his dwindling cash,
He whispered softly, “That was brash…”

So listen well, don’t be like Tim,
Who thought the knife would work for him.
For markets bite and punish the rash
Patience, my friend, will save your cash!

“The Kingdom” Live Trade Breakdown Over 100% Gainer on SPY Puts

This setup started off with a clear plan and strong discipline. The initial entry was on the SPY $490 Put for 4/16, with a tight 10% stop-loss, which protected us from unnecessary drawdown during early market chop. We respected the stop after identifying too much indecision and chop in price action, a key move that kept emotions in check and capital safe.

Once the market provided better directional confirmation, specifically a clean break back into the Opening Range Breakdown (ORB), we re-entered the same contract lightly, keeping the 10% stop in place.

From there, the trade setup played out beautifully:
	•	SPY broke below the ORB, retested it properly, and confirmed resistance at prior support.
	•	Momentum picked up, and we rode the wave down through multiple support levels.
	•	Entry was around SPY $525, and we scaled out into strength as it dropped.
	•	Final stop hit in profit at $518.67, locking in over 100% gain.

Some members with diamond hands held even longer and took profits at SPY $510, banking even more from the move.

This trade is a perfect example of:
	•	Taking disciplined losses early
	•	Re-entering only with confirmation
	•	Letting the trade work and respecting structure
	•	And most importantly locking in great gains

### Post #867: 2025-04-16 05:53

**Why Confluence Trading Works**

Think of confluence trading like baking cookies.
Example: 

	•	an MA trader puts the cookies in the oven when the timer hits 10 minutes. ⏲️

	•	a Fib trader puts them in when the dough looks shiny. ✨ 

	•	a VWAP trader goes by the smell in the kitchen. 👃

Each one uses just one signal (or reason) to bake.

But a confluence trader waits until:
	•	The timer says 10 minutes, ⏲️
	•	The dough looks shiny, and ✨
	•	The cookies smell perfect.  👃


Why?
Because using all three signs together means the cookies are way more likely to come out perfect.

Same in trading: confluence means waiting for all signs to agree, so your trade has a better chance in playing in your favor. 





*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/Lfk/why-confluence-trading-works*

### Post #895: 2025-04-27 13:36

**Be still like water, patient like the brush. Let the market paint its picture fully before you decide where to leave your mark.**

        •	   Be still like water: Stay calm and flexible, not forcing anything. Water moves only when necessary and adjusts to what’s around it just like a good trader should.
	•	Patient like the brush: An artist doesn’t rush when they paint. They move the brush carefully, waiting for the right moment. As a trader, you should wait for the right setup before making a move.
	•	Let the market paint its picture fully: Don’t jump in early. Let price action tell its full story (show the structure, liquidity, imbalance, confirmation) before you act.
	•	Decide where to leave your mark: After the picture is clear, then and only then should you enter your trade leaving your “mark” with precision.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/L7Z/be-still-like-water-patient-li*

### Post #923: 2025-05-03 00:59 — `$SPY` `[Gain]` `[Gain]`

**$SPY 5/2 The Resistance**

Today’s play showed a beautiful example of how resistance works. And what happens when it breaks. Before the breakout, price tapped the same resistance level four times (highlighted in orange circles). Each tap showed diminishing rejection and stronger closes near the level classic buildup behavior. According to the 4-tap rule, the more times a level is tested, the weaker it becomes. By the fourth tap, sellers were exhausted, and buyers were ready to reclaim control. This setup was a textbook example of liquidity stacking and pressure build-up leading to an explosive breakout once the level gave out. Great observations go a long way! HAVE A GREAT WEEKEND AFTERHOUR FAMILY ❤️🥂

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/MLi/dollarspy-52-the-resistance*

### Post #924: 2025-05-05 01:20

**“Finding Your Identity as a Trader” – A Message from TrynaMakeIt 👑**

One of the biggest breakthroughs in my trading journey wasn’t catching a perfect move it was finally understanding what kind of trader I am.
Not knowing that kept me in a loop: strategy-hopping, inconsistent results, and mental burnout. Once I figured it out, everything changed.


So, what kind of trader am I?

I’m a confluence-based intraday trader who builds trade ideas around multiple layers of confirmation. My system is rooted in structure and refined by precision.

My core tools and concepts:
	•	Break of Structure (BOS) – for directional bias and trend confirmation
	•	Fair Value Gaps (FVGs) – to mark imbalance and high-probability mitigation zones
	•	AVWAP (Anchored VWAP) – for volume-weighted control points and dynamic S/R
	•	EMA convergence – to confirm momentum and trend alignment
	•	Fibonacci retracement - to locate optimal pullback entries within structure
	•	RSI divergence - to spot weakening momentum or hidden continuation setups

I wait for these elements to align. No single indicator drives the trade it’s the confluence of structure, price, volume, and momentum that creates the edge.


How I discovered this:
	1.	I tried everything. Scalping, swing trading, indicator-heavy systems, pure price action. None clicked long-term.
	2.	I tracked what made sense to me. The setups I could explain logically and execute calmly that’s what I leaned into.
	3.	I studied my winners and losers. I saw the patterns in the trades that worked they weren’t random. They had confluence.
	4.	I let go of noise. Once I found my process, I stopped chasing and started refining.



Why this matters for you:

If you don’t know your style, you’ll constantly feel:
	•	Frustrated by others’ success.
	•	Confused by too much information.
	•	Inconsistent in results and emotions.

You’ll be busy but lost until you define who you are as a trader.


How to find your trading identity:
	•	Experiment early, but track everything.
	•	Identify what fits your psychology and lifestyle.
	•	Double down on clarity and consistency.
	•	Find setups that feel “clean” to you not forced.
	•	Create a checklist. Stick to it. Refine over time.


I’m not here to catch every move. I’m here to master my edge. 🥂

That only became possible once I knew exactly what kind of trader I was. 🔬 

Now my question to you is. What kind of trader are you?

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/MQC/finding-your-identity-as-a-tra*

### Post #925: 2025-05-05 02:41

**Let’s Talk About “Beginner’s Luck”. My Transparent Trading Story (Late March 2024 - Early June 2024 {The Arc Began})**

When I first started trading, I didn’t know much.

No real system. No deep understanding of structure. Barely any psychology work.
But somehow… I turned $755 into $3,200 in under 2 months.

Call it momentum. Call it adrenaline. Call it “beginner’s luck.”

⸻

But then, I lost it all.

All the gains. Gone.
Why?

Because the truth is: I didn’t know what I was doing.
I had no risk management. No discipline. No understanding of the market’s rhythm.
Just a small winning streak that inflated my ego and blinded me.

⸻

Here’s what that experience taught me:
	•	The market isn’t a game it’s a mirror.
It reflects your level of discipline, knowledge, and emotional control. I had none.
	•	Big gains mean nothing if they’re not repeatable.
That $3.2k didn’t mean I was a good trader. It just meant I got lucky before the truth caught up.
	•	Freedom doesn’t come from fast money it comes from consistency.
Losing that money was the moment I realized: this market is real.
It’s not just numbers. It’s a path to financial freedom but only for those who respect it.

⸻

Where I’m at now:

I don’t make those “crazy gains” every day anymore.
I’ve shifted my focus entirely:
	•	From fast flips to compounded consistency
	•	From hopeful trades to confluence and structure-based setups
	•	From gambling to growth

If you’ve had beginner’s luck enjoy it, but don’t mistake it for mastery.
And if you’ve lost everything after a streak good. That’s the real beginning.

The market will humble you before it rewards you.
I chose to stay, learn, adapt  and that’s made all the difference.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/MQ1/lets-talk-about-beginners-luck*

### Post #931: 2025-05-05 18:44

**Going Short Based on Feelings is a Fast Track to Blowing Your Account**

To anyone shorting SPY on every dollar move up from 561 this morning this is for you 🥂❤️


Shorting the market just because it “feels too high” or “shouldn’t be going up” is a dangerous mindset. The market doesn’t care about opinions, emotions, or what seems “logical” to you.

Price action and technicals tell the real story.
Ignoring them to chase a bias usually leads to frustration, overtrading, and losses. If you find yourself shorting just because you’re convinced the market has to drop, pause and ask:
	•	What is the chart actually saying?
	•	Am I reacting to price or trying to predict it emotionally?

Discipline > ego. Trade what you see, not what you feel.

### Post #944: 2025-05-08 22:04 — `$SPY`

**$SPY The Doji Candle**

Timeframe : Daily

A doji candle forms when the open and close prices are nearly the same, creating a small or non-existent body. A red doji means the close was slightly below the open — bearish bias, but still mostly indecisive.

⸻

What it Means:
	•	Indecision between buyers and sellers.
	•	Market tried to move in one or both directions but failed to close far from the open.
	•	Momentum is stalling — especially after a strong trend.

⸻

Effect on Price:
	•	Often a pause or potential reversal is coming.
	•	By itself, it’s not a strong signal — needs confirmation from the next candle(s).
	•	In an uptrend, a red doji shows buyers may be losing control.

⸻

If It Appears on a Daily Chart at a High Point:
	•	This is where it gets important:
	•	Suggests buying pressure is weakening.
	•	Potential reversal or short-term pullback could be near.

A red doji at a high is a warning sign that the uptrend may be stalling. It’s a signal to be cautious with longs and to watch for bearish confirmation in the next session. Continuation = invalidation 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/NeO/dollarspy-the-doji-candle*

### Post #952: 2025-05-17 01:14

**Hybrid Markets, What are they? And how should you approach it.**

A hybrid market refers to a market environment that combines features of both trending and range-bound conditions. It’s one of the most difficult to trade.

•	Grinds upward or downward slowly but still respects short-term ranges
	•	Has inconsistent follow-through on breakouts and breakdowns
	•	Shows false reversals (divergences form, but price keeps grinding)
	•	Whipsaws intraday, yet still trends on higher timeframes
	•	Looks extended, but refuses to correct the way you’d expect

🛠 How to Adapt

1. Reduce Size. Hybrid markets punish size and reward precision.

2. Stay With the Trend, But Don’t Chase.

3. Early trades = traps. Wait until 10:00–10:15 AM for a cleaner read.

4. Use Higher Timeframe Anchors.

5. Focus on Reaction, Not Prediction.

⚖️ Hybrid Mindset 💡

“If I treat this like a trending market, I’ll get chopped. If I treat this like a choppy market, I’ll miss the trend. I must let price prove itself before I commit.”

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/N6T/hybrid-markets-what-are-they-a*

**Little Trade Recap through the storm (Showcasing the technicals for you guys)**

📈 Trade Recap — SPY 605c 6/23 @ 1.57 Entry | TP 601.5 | SL 10%

Entry Thesis:
Caught a high-conviction momentum move after a key bullish market structure shift and demand zone respect.

🥂 Structure Flip (ChoCH): Price broke previous intraday structure highs, confirming a shift from bearish to bullish on the 5-min.

🔹 Demand Zone Reaction: Entry followed a clean bounce off the 598.20–596.35 gap area — demand held strong and pushed price higher.

🔹 Break + Retest of PMH: Previous Mid-High flipped into support; confirmation entry came on breakout with strong volume.

🔹 RSI & Volume Confirmation: RSI pushed above 70 as volume surged — clear momentum confirmation on both 5m and 15m charts.

Target:
601.5 — just under PDH (601.74) to front-run potential liquidity grabs and take profit into strength.

Stop Loss:
10% — tight risk, invalidation would’ve been a breakdown back below PMH or key fib levels (599.33 area).

⸻

🧠 Why It Worked:
	•	Structure flipped cleanly
	•	Demand was respected
	•	Breakout had momentum and volume
	•	Target was logical, just under a key liquidity level

Monthly to Weekly to Daily Breakdown**

📅 Monthly Chart – Big Picture Shift

Triple bottom formed over a three-year span with rising lows showing buyers stepping in stronger each cycle
Price broke out of a multi-year falling wedge that started from the 2019 all-time highs
This breakout wasn’t soft it was a full structure reclaim above the wedge and the 210 neckline zone
Monthly RSI flipped the script and reclaimed 50 for the first time in years, confirming a major shift in momentum

Targets
233 to 243 is the first structural shelf with heavy historical price memory
268 and beyond is where the real supply lives from the 2021 distribution zone

🧭 Weekly Chart – Structure Reversal Confirmed

A clean double bottom off macro trendline support broke through the neckline at 196
Downtrend resistance is now acting as confirmed support after a successful retest of that neckline 
RSI broke its bearish structure and is climbing with strength, printing a bullish divergence along the way
Volume confirms the move, expanding as price lifted off key levels

📆 Daily Chart – Execution Zone

Price filled a clean imbalance at 206 to 212 and exploded through it with strength
Volume surged and confirmed conviction at the breakout

Smart money wasn’t chasing highs. They accumulated on the second low of the double bottom and are now driving price with momentum (currently $214) 

🚀 Final Thoughts

Boeing has now shifted structure across all timeframes
The triple bottom gave it a launchpad
The wedge breakout gave it a direction
The RSI and volume gave it confirmation
And the price action gave it conviction

As long as bulls defend the 210 to 200 range, this has room to grind and squeeze into 268 and potentially higher by year-end

### Post #1067: 2025-07-20 22:45 — `$SPY` `[Chart]`

**$SPY**

We’re currently watching a potential bull flag formation on the SPY 1H chart, with multiple confluences aligning in favor of a short-term continuation to the upside, targeting 630.

🔹 Key Bullish Confluences:
	•	Bull Flag Pattern: Price is consolidating in a tight range with lower highs and higher lows after a strong impulse leg — typical flag structure.
	•	FVG Support Zone: Price is holding inside a bullish Fair Value Gap (FVG)
	•	.382 Fib Retracement: The .382 Fibonacci level at ~627.45 is holding well as support a common continuation retracement zone in bullish momentum.

🔻 Bearish Overlay (Daily Chart Context)

While the 1H chart shows a strong potential continuation setup, the daily timeframe just printed a Hanging Man candle, which throws a caution flag.

🛑 What the Hanging Man Tells Us:
	•	Occurs after an extended bullish move.
	•	Suggests buyer exhaustion and potential short-term reversal or pullback.
	•	The small real body at the top of the range with a long lower wick shows intraday selling pressure that buyers had to recover from, warning sign.
	•	If the next daily candle closes below 626.46 (the Hanging Man’s low), it could confirm the reversal and invalidate the bull flag breakout.


Lows target 623.33 high $630

### Post #1072: 2025-07-23 18:23

**Quick Lesson (STOP HUNTING) 🥂**


📊 Breakdown of the Chart

    📍 Key Resistance at $632.79

        This red horizontal line marks a previous high.

        A price level where many retail traders likely placed stop-loss orders (above short entries) or buy stop orders to enter a breakout.

    📈 Break of Structure (BoS)

        You can see that price broke above a previous high.

        This is typically interpreted as a breakout but in many cases, this is where stop hunts occur.

    🟦 Price Wicks Above High ("Stop Hunt")

        The blue label and arrow show price slightly breaking above $632.79 just enough to trigger stop losses. 🪤

        This often represents the market collecting liquidity. 💰

    📉 Immediate Reversal

        After hitting the weak high, the price quickly reverses a classic sign of a stop hunt.

        Traders who bought the breakout are now in drawdown.🥲

    🔁 Fibonacci Retracement Levels

        After the stop hunt, the price is expected to pull back to one of the key fib levels (0.382, 0.618, 0.706).

        These levels are commonly used to find re-entry zones after the hunt. 👀

    📉 Volume Spike

        Notice the volume spike during the wick.

        This indicates a sudden burst of buy-side activity (stops triggered), not genuine interest.

Quick lesson since this happened live today! Don’t get caught with these 🪤 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/UwX/quick-lesson-stop-hunting*

### Post #1092: 2025-07-30 19:14

**How I Catch Clean Trades 🧠➡️📉📈
Strip the noise. Stick to structure. 👑**


Here’s the simple formula I use to catch consistent, high-probability trades no screeners no fancy programs just trading view tools. (Free) 


🔹 Step 1: Liquidity Sweep
I wait for price to take out a key high or low 
aka stop hunt / liquidity grab. (higher time frame levels pre market levels etc) 

📌 This tells me where dumb money just got trapped.


🔹 Step 2: Look for the FVG (Fair Value Gap)
Right after the sweep, I mark the imbalance 
the 3-candle price inefficiency left behind.

📌 That’s where smart money wants price to return to.


🔹 Step 3: Deep Fib Pullback
I draw a Fib from the impulse move (after the sweep)
and wait for price to retrace into the .618–.786 zone,
inside the FVG.

📌 When price lands there → I lock in.

⸻

🔹 Entry + SL/TP
	•	Entry: at the FVG inside the .618–.786 zone
	•	SL: just below the sweep low/high
	•	TP: the opposite side of the range or next liquidity level

⸻

✅ That’s it.
No indicators.
No overthinking.
Just liquidity → imbalance → fib trap zone.


📈 If it doesn’t sweep, I don’t trade.
📉 If it doesn’t give me a deep retrace into the FVG, I wait.

Patience > Prediction.
Trap the trapper.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/VlP/how-i-catch-clean-trades-strip*

### Post #1105: 2025-08-13 02:30

**How To Spot Trends 👑**

6 Easy Ways to Spot a Trend

📈 Trading Patterns – Look for breakouts from shapes like wedges, triangles, basing patterns.

📈 Moving Average – Price staying above a moving average often signals an uptrend.

📈 Trading Channels – Price bouncing inside an upward-sloping channel means momentum is intact.

📈 Support/Resistance – Higher lows and breaking past old highs show strength.

📈 Fibonacci Levels – Pullbacks to key Fib zones (38.2%, 50%, 61.8%) can be launch points.

📈 Volume – Increasing volume confirms the move is real, not just a fake push.

The more of these signals you see lining up, the stronger the trend.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/WJd/how-to-spot-trends*

### Post #1113: 2025-08-20 12:33

**For my Daytraders**

Why Volume Matters More than Open Interest in Day Trading Options

A lot of traders put too much weight on open interest (OI) when making short-term options trades. The truth is, volume is way more important if you’re day trading.
	•	Open Interest (OI): This shows how many contracts exist from the previous trading sessions. It’s basically a running tally of positions that are still open. It doesn’t tell you if those positions were bullish or bearish, or if they were opened days/weeks ago. So if you base your trade off OI, you might just end up bag-holding with the crowd that bought earlier while the stock goes the other direction.
	•	Volume: This shows how many contracts are being traded right now, today. High volume means there’s active interest, liquidity, and tighter spreads all crucial if you’re scalping or day trading. Volume reflects today’s sentiment and flow, which is what actually moves the option price in real time.

The Misconception:
A lot of traders see big OI and think it means “smart money is here, I should be too.” The problem is those contracts could have been bought days or weeks ago at totally different price levels and intentions. If you follow blindly, you’re not trading the current move you’re just holding the bag with stale contracts.

The Key Takeaway:
For day trading, focus on volume + price action of the stock. Open interest is useful for understanding longer-term positioning and liquidity, but it won’t save you intraday. The traders who confuse the two usually end up stuck while the market leaves them behind. 💯


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/Xqv/for-my-daytraders*

### Post #1114: 2025-08-22 01:28

**Don’t Be Strictly Bullish or Bearish Trade the Trend at Hand.**

One of the biggest mistakes traders make is locking themselves into a permanent bias. Markets don’t move in one direction forever, and neither should your mindset.

Key mindset shift: Stop identifying as “bullish” or “bearish.” Instead, identify as a trend trader.
	•	If the trend is up, ride it.
	•	If the trend shifts, adjust with it.
	•	If conditions are unclear, protect your capital and wait.

Being aware of trend changes is where many traders either succeed or fail. Don’t ignore the signs: momentum shifts, volume patterns, higher timeframe support/resistance, or breaks in structure. The earlier you recognize them, the better you can position yourself.

💡 Remember: Markets reward flexibility, not stubbornness. Take TSSI for example the herd followed a sheep in to the slaughterhouse catching a falling knife from a $26 alert just to have TSSI end at $15. No strategy no solidified reasoning just trying to be a perma bull at every dip given and it’s not smart.

Trade what the market gives you not what you want it to give.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/XCe/dont-be-strictly-bullish-or-be*

### Post #1129: 2025-09-16 04:22

**🚨 “Sell the News” vs. “Buy the News” What It Really Means 🚨**

You’ve probably heard the phrase “sell the news” when a stock tanks right after positive earnings… or “buy the news” when a surprise announcement sparks a rally.

Here’s the truth: markets don’t move on the news itself they move on the gap between expectations and reality.

🔑 Before the news:
Markets are forward-looking. Traders and institutions often position ahead of earnings, Fed meetings, CPI prints, product launches, etc. The more predictable the event, the more it’s already priced in.

🔑 During the news:
The reaction depends on whether the outcome is better, worse, or exactly what was expected.

🔑 After the news:
Profit-taking, repositioning, or trend continuation sets in. Sometimes the second-order effects matter more (ex: not just “Fed cut rates,” but what that signals about the economy 6 months out).

📌 Examples:
	•	Earnings: Stock rallies for weeks, crushes expectations… then drops because optimism was already priced in → Sell the news.
	•	CPI report: Inflation comes in cooler than expected → surprise buying because fears were overblown → Buy the news.
	•	Crypto/Tech hype: ETFs, halvings, product launches often rally in advance… then dip once the event is official → Sell the news.

💡 Takeaways for traders:
	•	Watch implied volatility → options often reveal how much movement is already expected.
	•	Pay attention to positioning before the event → a heavy run-up = higher risk of sell-the-news.
	•	Trade the expectation gap, not the headline.

👉 Bottom line: The market is a discounting machine. Events move price only if they shock expectations. That’s why understanding what’s already priced in is just as important as knowing the news itself.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/0gN/sell-the-news-vs-buy-the-news-*

✅ Already integrated everywhere (ChatGPT, self-driving, finance, medicine, productivity)
✅ Billions flowing into GPUs & chips = future infrastructure

### Post #1170: 2025-09-30 19:26

**Market Shutdowns. The facts vs The noise**

1. Historical Market Data During Shutdowns
	•	Since 1976, the U.S. has had 20 government shutdowns.
	•	Median S&P 500 performance during shutdowns = basically flat (slight losses of ~0.1–0.5%).
	•	After shutdowns end, the market usually rallies. For example:
	•	2013 (16-day shutdown) → S&P 500 was up +3% during, and finished the year up +30%.
	•	2018/2019 (35-day shutdown) → Market went up +11% in the next 3 months.
	•	Shutdowns are political theater, not an economic collapse trigger.

2. Why expecting a “crash” off of this makes no sense 
	•	Markets price in expectations. Everyone knows a shutdown is looming, so it’s not a surprise event.
	•	Shutdowns = temporary disruption. Workers get back pay, programs resume. It doesn’t destroy earnings or economic fundamentals.
	•	Liquidity and global demand for U.S. assets don’t vanish overnight because Congress bickers.
	•	Crashes require systemic shocks (like 2008’s credit freeze, COVID shutdown of global economy, or unexpected Black Swan). A scheduled, highly publicized government drama isn’t that.

3. What Actually Moves Markets in Shutdowns
	•	Short-term volatility in certain sectors (defense contractors, government contractors, travel).
	•	Bond yields may wobble if debt ceiling debates overlap.
	•	Investor psychology (headlines = fear, but smart money buys dips).


👉 Bottom line: historically, shutdowns = noise, not collapse. Thinking let me full port puts over something that has happened 20 times before without causing systemic collapse is just fear talking, not facts. 💯

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/2ET/market-shutdowns-the-facts-vs-*

### Post #1237: 2025-11-02 22:44

**Time to be frank 💯**

Let’s be real nothing of value is free in this world and not all paid Discords are created equal. But the ones that genuinely provide value are worth every dollar. 

Free servers often make *you* the product they collect data, push affiliate links, or feed off your engagement without actually giving you structure, mentorship, or results.  

Now compare that to a community where $30/month gives you:  
- Full access to someone’s years of knowledge  
- Daily livestreams breaking down the markets in real time  
- Countless active members helping each other grow  

That’s not just a chatroom  that’s a classroom, a support system, and a mentorship rolled into one.  

The real difference? One is built on *value and work ethic*. The others are built on *vibes and excuses.*  

So before you call every paid Discord a “scam,” look at what you’re getting. Some charge $100 just to be “friends” others charge $30 to teach you how to actually win.  My Kingdom speaks for itself and the members there can vouch. I don’t need the money which is why I charge very little for my time and efforts / the fee is a mere commitment phase. 


Anyone hating on this is better off trading on their own or are just trying to find a quick buck lol. Regardless the kingdom is not for you if you don’t need us 💯

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/8zf/time-to-be-frank*

### Post #1366: 2025-12-05 23:18 — `$CVNA, $SPY`

**$SPY**

Incase your wondering

$CVNA likely makes up well under 0.2% of the S&P 500 probably in the ballpark of 0.1–0.15%

When a stock gets added:
	•	S&P 500 ETFs & index funds must buy it
	•	That creates a temporary demand spike
	•	That’s it — no magic after that

Once that forced buying is:
	•	✅ Priced in
	•	✅ Completed
There’s no ongoing structural tailwind anymore.

By the time retail is hyped:
	•	Institutions already accumulated
	•	Liquidity normalized
	•	Edge is gone


Nothing burger

### Post #1418: 2025-12-19 01:25

**BoJ / Japan Carry Trade Summary**


Japan has very low interest rates, so big investors borrow cheap yen and use it to buy U.S. stocks, tech, and crypto. This is called the Japan carry trade.

When the Bank of Japan hints at higher rates:
	•	The yen strengthens
	•	Borrowing gets more expensive
	•	Investors rush to sell U.S. assets to pay back yen loans
	•	Markets drop, often overnight

This is what happened during the 2024 carry trade dump U.S. futures sold off before NY opened, catching retail off guard.

Key rule:
	•	Weak yen = risk ON (stocks up)
	•	Strong yen = risk OFF (stocks down)

BoJ news hits at night (US time) and often sets the tone for the next U.S. session.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bc48/boj-japan-carry-trade-summary*

### Post #1419: 2025-12-19 02:08

**December Opex tomorrow**

OPEX = Options Expiration Day
	•	Happens every Friday, sometimes bigger on “triple witching” days when stock, index, and futures options all expire.
	•	On OPEX, a lot of options contracts expire, meaning traders who bought them either cash out, roll them over, or get assigned.
	•	This can push stock prices around because big players hedge or unwind positions to avoid losses.
	•	Prices often move toward the “max pain” level, where the fewest options expire in the money — basically the level that hurts the fewest traders financially.
	•	Result: higher volume, bigger swings, sometimes fake moves, then normal trading resumes after.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bc5e/december-opex-tomorrow*

### Post #1444: 2025-12-26 00:27

**A moment to share my passion for TA (Technical Analysis)**

When approached properly, TA isn’t random lines on a screen or abstract theory. It’s rhythm, tension, and release. It’s watching price breathe, feeling when momentum tightens, and recognizing when something is about to give. Candles tell stories. Structure leaves scars. Levels exist because memory exists, and the market never forgets where emotion once lived.

Technical analysis often gets looked down on, usually for a few familiar reasons.

It’s easy to misuse. Many people rush it, overcrowd their charts, ignore higher timeframe context, and chase every flicker of movement. When it falls apart, frustration replaces reflection, and TA becomes the scapegoat instead of discipline.

It demands accountability. The chart doesn’t lie or soften the truth. If the plan was clear and emotion took over, the evidence is right there, candle by candle.

And most people crave certainty. TA offers probability, not comfort. It teaches patience, restraint, and acceptance of uncertainty, which can feel uncomfortable in a world that wants instant answers.

The reality is this: every consistently profitable trader eventually carves out their own language with the market. Some lean technical. Some lean fundamental. Some feel flow, sentiment, or macro pressure. All of it is data. All of it matters.

The danger isn’t choosing one path. The danger is closing yourself off.

The market is alive. It’s layered, emotional, and adaptive. Turning your back on learning because something doesn’t match your current style only limits your vision.

Study everything. Respect every method. Let your eyes learn patterns, let your mind understand context, and let experience shape your intuition.

That’s how consistency is built.
Not by arguing which method is right,
but by learning how to truly read what’s in front of you.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bdvC/a-moment-to-share-my-passion-f*

### Post #1448: 2025-12-26 18:19 — `$SPY`

**Little sneak peak into my brain $SPY**

Price is currently trading below the 50 SMA at 690.29 (yellow EMA) while holding the 200 EMA (red EMA, not the horizontal level) after reacting off a previously respected support zone (purple box within the larger purple range).

This structure sets up a high-probability continuation if bulls step in.

🔍 Technical Confluence

1️⃣ Falling Wedge (Bullish Reversal Pattern)
Price has been compressing into a falling wedge — lower highs and lower lows with declining downside momentum. This pattern statistically favors upside resolution, especially when it forms at support and into a key moving average.

2️⃣ Bullish RSI Divergence
RSI is printing higher lows while price made lower lows, signaling bear exhaustion and a shift in momentum. This tells us sellers are losing control even though price hasn’t fully reacted yet often the early warning before expansion.

📈 Trigger
	•	Bullish continuation once SPY reclaims and holds above the 50 SMA at 690.29
	•	Acceptance above this level opens the door for momentum expansion toward HOD

❌ Invalidation
	•	Breakdown and acceptance below 689.65
	•	Loss of this level invalidates the wedge + divergence thesis and shifts bias back to downside continuation

🧠 Why This Setup Matters

This isn’t just a moving-average cross.
It’s structure + momentum + support alignment:
	•	Compression → Expansion
	•	Momentum divergence → Directional shift
	•	Support hold → Defined risk

Let price confirm.
Strength above 690.29 = opportunity.
Loss of 689.65 = thesis broken.

Trade what’s confirmed, not what’s hoped.

### Post #1452: 2025-12-31 01:32

**How to pick a stock for new investors**

Qualitative analysis

 is about understanding the company itself:
– What does the company do, and how does it make money?
– Are earnings growing or shrinking over time?
– How much debt does the company carry, and can it comfortably manage it?
– Is management making smart long-term decisions?
– Does the business have a real advantage or strong demand?

This helps answer the most important question for investors: Is this a healthy business I can trust long-term?

Quantitative analysis 

supports this by looking at measurable data:
– Revenue and earnings trends
– Profit margins
– Debt levels relative to income
– Valuation metrics (like P/E or cash flow)

Combining these aspects before hitting that buy button can heighten your chances of staying in a position and maximizing profits! 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bdLZ/how-to-pick-a-stock-for-new-in*

### Post #1475: 2026-01-03 15:30

**My opinion on the situation politics aside**

As someone who has close friends of family who have been directly affected by Maduro’s regime, I don’t speak on Venezuela from theory or headlines. I speak from lived reality.

For years, Venezuela was run under an authoritarian system that crushed opposition, controlled institutions, silenced dissent, and forced millions to flee just to survive. People lost access to basic necessities, life savings were destroyed by hyperinflation, and speaking out often came with real consequences. That isn’t politics, that’s human suffering.

Because of that context, I believe the pressure applied under Trump may have been one of the best things to happen to Venezuela in recent history. Not because it was perfect, but because it finally placed real consequences on a regime that had operated without accountability for years. It challenged the regime’s legitimacy, exposed corruption internationally, and signaled to Venezuelans that the world hadn’t completely turned its back on them.

Venezuela did not collapse by accident. It was the result of centralized power, broken institutions, and a system that functioned as a dictatorship rather than a democracy. Today, the country is no longer operating under the same unchecked structure it once was, and any action that disrupted that grip mattered.

This isn’t about loyalty to any politician. It’s about acknowledging when a decision aligns with the lived reality of people who suffered under that system. If you’ve never watched your country collapse from the inside, it’s easy to oversimplify. If you have loved ones who lived it, you understand why that moment mattered.

Freedom and Power to the people of Venezuela may their future be bright and filled with grace 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bd3s/my-opinion-on-the-situation-po*

### Post #1487: 2026-01-10 18:07

**Obsession has no off switch 📵**

If yours does you were never obsessed, you just had a hobby with good lighting. It’s not something you squeeze in after work. It’s the reason you wake up, it’s the voice in your head that never shuts up.

It’s when the thing you want most stops being a goal and starts becoming your operating system. You don’t think about it sometimes you see everything through it. 

The goal doesn’t just ask for your attention. It owns it. The problem is that most people treat their dream like a side hustle. They check in when it’s convenient, show up when they feel like it, and work on it when they have time. 

The ones who succeed in every level don’t visit their dreams they live inside them. 

No clock outs
No reminders
No motivation mondays
Or New Year’s resolutions 

Every choice, every sacrifice, every no is all filtered through that one question. “Does this serve the mission?”

The obsessed can’t sleep because the vision is to loud. They feel more alive working toward the goal than they do relaxing without it.

If you can turn it off it was never yours.
If you can walk away it was never real.

If it haunts you
If it drives you
If it owns you

Than you’re exactly where you need to be. Obsession has no off switch, and the ones who win stopped looking for one a long time ago.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/beLS/obsession-has-no-off-switch*

### Post #1512: 2026-01-18 16:01

**How to Pick the RIGHT Options Contract (Using the Greeks) 🇬🇷🇬🇷
Straight from the Notes 📝**

Options aren’t just about direction. 📈📉

Two traders can be right on direction and still lose! contract selection matters!!!!!

This is where the Greeks come in. 🥂

1️⃣ Delta (Δ) — Direction & Strength 💪

What it tells you:
How much your option moves for every $1 move in the stock.

Example:
Delta = 0.50 → stock moves $1 → option moves ~$0.50

How to use it:
	•	Higher Delta = option behaves more like shares
	•	Delta ≈ probability of finishing ITM
	•	Day traders/scalpers: look for 0.6–0.8 Delta
	•	Lower Delta = cheaper but needs a bigger move


2️⃣ Gamma (Γ) — Speed of the Move ⚡️

What it tells you:
How fast Delta changes when price moves.

Why it matters:
	•	High Gamma = fast P&L swings
	•	Most aggressive near-the-money & close to expiration

Example:
Gamma = 0.05 → $1 move → Delta jumps from 0.50 → 0.55

How to use it:
	•	Great for quick momentum trades
	•	Dangerous for holding overnight
	•	Expect volatility in P&L


3️⃣ Theta (Θ) — Time Is the Enemy ⏰

What it tells you:
How much value your option loses per day.
	•	Always negative for buyers

Example:
Theta = –0.08 → loses $0.08/day automatically

How to use it:
	•	Buyers want slower Theta
	•	Avoid buying contracts with heavy decay unless scalping
	•	20–60 DTE = safer for swings


4️⃣ Vega (ν) — Volatility Risk ⚔️

What it tells you:
How much option price changes when IV moves 1%.

Example:
Vega = 0.12 → IV +1% = option +$0.12

How to use it:
	•	High Vega = expensive options
	•	Earnings = IV spike (bad for buyers after)
	•	IV crush hurts longs
	•	Sellers LOVE high Vega


5️⃣ Rho (ρ) — Interest Rates (Minor)

What it tells you:
Impact of interest rates on option value.

Reality:
	•	Mostly irrelevant for day trades
	•	Matters more for LEAPS

🎯 Contract Selection Cheat Code

✅ Day Trading / Scalping
	•	Delta: 0.6–0.8
	•	Expect high Gamma
	•	Accept Theta burn

✅ Swing Trading
	•	20–60 DTE
	•	Moderate Delta
	•	Controlled Theta

✅ Before Earnings
	•	Be careful buying (Vega risk)
	•	IV usually collapses after

✅ Selling Premium
	•	High Theta + High Vega
	•	Time & IV work for you

🚀 One-Line Cheat Sheet
	•	Delta = Direction
	•	Gamma = Acceleration
	•	Theta = Time decay
	•	Vega = Volatility risk
	•	Rho = Rates (least important)




*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bfu2/how-to-pick-the-right-options-*

### Post #1514: 2026-01-18 21:49

**Analysis Paralysis Is Killing Your Trades 🧠**

If you’ve ever:
• Seen the setup
• Done the analysis
• Hesitated…
• Watched price move without you

That’s analysis paralysis. 🥶

Markets don’t reward certainty they reward decisive probability.

The human mind is conditioned for certainty. In order to be a successful trader shift that to accept and endure probability 💯

You will never have:
• Every indicator aligned
• Perfect timing
• Zero risk

Waiting for perfection = no trades. 🤷🏽‍♂️

How to beat it:
1️⃣ Limit inputs (1–2 indicators, clear timeframe)
2️⃣ Plan entry, stop, target before you enter
3️⃣ Trade scenarios, not predictions
4️⃣ Accept imperfect entries with controlled risk

Confidence comes from repetition, not more analysis. 💯

Simplify. Execute. Repeat. 🎯

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bfwn/analysis-paralysis-is-killing-*

### Post #1519: 2026-01-19 23:02 `[Discuss]`

**Supply + Demand (From a TA nerd)**

Alright AfterHour Fam to start this off I have 4 images below that will be used as reference throughout this lesson. Image 4 is a demand example. Complete opposite of supply. Lets start off with,

🥪 Understanding the Supply Zone (Purple Box reference image 1 and 2)
(Image 3 identifies supply in the form of a bearish FVG) 

The top purple box marks a supply zone an area where sellers stepped in aggressively before.

Think of it like this:
	•	Buyers push price up
	•	Sellers say “this is expensive”
	•	Price gets rejected

That rejection leaves behind unfilled sell orders.
That’s what creates supply.

⛓️‍💥 What Happens on Repeated Tests

Each time price comes back into a supply zone:
	•	Sellers have to defend again
	•	Some sell orders get filled
	•	Liquidity gets used up

Therefore, Supply is not unlimited. This is where the Rule of 4 comes in.

📙 The Rule of 4 

Every retest of a level makes it weaker.

• First touch → strong reaction
• Second touch → still defended
• Third touch → weaker bounce
• Fourth touch → probability shifts toward failure

Markets don’t break levels randomly 
they break them when orders are exhausted.

📊 Now let’s look deeper into these charts.

On the first 3 images. 
	•	Price failed to hold above the supply zone
	•	Price failed to make new highs
	•	Multiple wicks formed into resistance

This tells us buyers were trying… but couldn’t take control.

That’s not strength that’s distribution 📉📉

🥨 Midpoint Support Getting Weak (reference image 1) 

The green (Previous Hour low) midpoint acted as temporary support at first.

But notice: (reference image 2)
	•	Each bounce got smaller
	•	Price kept coming back to the same level
	•	Buyers needed more effort just to hold it

Buyers are getting tired.

🥐 Momentum Confirms the Story

RSI wasn’t holding bullish strength:
	•	Lower highs formed
	•	Momentum rolled over
	•	Buyers failed to reclaim control

When structure + momentum agree, probability increases.


🥖 The Result

Once midpoint support finally failed:
	•	Price dropped quickly into the lower demand zone
	•	No chop
	•	No hesitation

That’s what happens when a level breaks due to order exhaustion.

🔁 Vice Versa: How This Builds Support

The concept of how supply is identified works both ways.

When price repeatedly taps a support without breaking down that is known as demand zone:
	•	Sellers get absorbed
	•	Downside momentum weakens
	•	Bounces become stronger

By the 4th tap into support:
	•	Probability shifts toward strength
	•	Breakouts higher become more likely

This is how real support is built not by guessing bottoms, but by watching how price reacts over time.

🥯 Demand + Example (Why Price Stopped Instantly at the lower purple box reference image 2)

Notice what happened once price tapped the lower purple box.

What price did…

	•	Tagged the demand zone
	•	Immediately found support
	•	Began stabilizing without extra   downside

That tells us…

	•	Buyers were already waiting
	•	Resting buy orders absorbed the sell  pressure
	•	Demand was fresh and untested

Strong demand often reacts fast the first touch matters most.


🧠 Key Takeaways (Keep These Simple)

✔ Repeated tests weaken levels
✔ Failure to make new highs is information
✔ The 4th test shifts probability
✔ Consolidation at resistance ≠ bullish
✔ Support and resistance are about order flow, not lines


In Conclusion 👑

Supply & demand isn’t about predicting the future.

It’s about recognizing when one side is running out of fuel.

Trade patience.
Trade structure.
Trade probability.

If you made it this far I hope you learned a thing or two! ✅

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bfyP/supply-demand-from-a-ta-nerd*

### Post #1548: 2026-01-27 20:19

**TA 101 (previous 3 posts combined)**

Let’s start with what is a Fair Value Gap (imbalance)?

A Fair Value Gap happens when price moves so aggressively that it leaves an imbalance between buyers and sellers. On the chart, this shows up as a 3-candle gap where the middle candle pushes hard and price doesn’t fully trade through the prior candle’s range.

👉 It’s basically inefficient pricing the market usually comes back to that zone to rebalance orders.

When the gap forms to the upside and price later respects it as support, that’s a Bullish FVG.


📍 What happened here

Price sold off into a previous bullish imbalance zone (the purple/green area) and instead of breaking down, it found buyers exactly where inefficiency existed before.

That tells us:
✔ Institutions previously stepped in here
✔ Orders were still resting
✔ The move down was likely just liquidity delivery into demand


🔥 Confluences that confirmed the reversal

This wasn’t just “gap = buy.” The edge came from stacked confirmation:

1️⃣ Bullish RSI Divergence

Price made a lower low, but RSI made a higher low.

That =
• Sellers losing momentum
• Downside pressure weakening
• Smart money absorption instead of continuation

Momentum disagreed with price → early reversal clue.


2️⃣ Inverse Head & Shoulders (Continuation Confirmation)

After bouncing from the FVG, price formed:

Left shoulder → Head → Right shoulder
Then broke structure to the upside.

That pattern told us:
🧠 The reaction wasn’t random
🧠 Buyers were defending higher lows
🧠 The move was shifting from reversal → continuation


🎯 Why this setup had high probability

You had:

✅ Higher timeframe imbalance (institutional footprint)
✅ Price returning to rebalance
✅ Momentum divergence
✅ Structure shift
✅ Continuation pattern (IHS)

💡 The lesson

Fair Value Gaps aren’t “magic boxes.”

They work best when:
Imbalance + Momentum Shift + Structure Confirmation align.

The FVG gives the location.
RSI divergence gives the condition.
The inverse H&S gives the trigger/confirmation.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bgol/ta-101-previous-3-posts-combin*

### Post #1561: 2026-01-31 03:22

**Are you a Sheep or Wolf? 🐑 🐺**

Are you the Predator or the Prey?

🐑 SHEEP THINKING (how most people trade)

Sheep react. Wolves plan.

Sheep wait for comfort.
Wolves act in discomfort.

Sheep think:
• “It’s going up, I’m safe now”
• “It’s dropping, I need to get out”
• “I’ll enter when it looks clear”

But here’s the truth that hurts:

👉 When price looks safe, it’s usually late.
👉 When it feels scary, that’s where opportunity lives.

Why?

Because sheep move together. And markets move against crowds to create liquidity.


🧠 What Sheep Don’t See

Sheep believe price moves because of:

• news
• candles
• indicators

Wolves know price moves because of:

liquidity hunts

The market doesn’t rise because it “wants to.”
It rises because orders are sitting above.

It doesn’t drop because it’s “bearish.”
It drops because stops are sitting below.

Retail trades direction.
Professionals trade where people will be wrong.


🐑 How Sheep Behave in Real Time

Price drops fast → sheep panic sell
Price rips after → sheep chase buy
Price chops → sheep overtrade
Price pulls back → sheep think trend is over

Their decisions come from:
fear, relief, hope, frustration

That emotional cycle is literally the fuel of the market.


🐺 How Wolves Think

Wolves ask one thing:

“Where are traders trapped?”

Not:
“Is this bullish or bearish?”

But:
“Who is uncomfortable right now?”

Because price moves toward pain.

Sheep need to be right.
Wolves need to manage risk.

Sheep exit at pain.
Wolves enter where pain is highest.

Sheep think:
“I hope this works”

Wolves think:
“If I’m wrong, I lose X. That’s fine.”

Which are you? Which will you be?



*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bgMl/are-you-a-sheep-or-wolf*

### Post #1567: 2026-02-01 01:27

**Little $DXY (USD) breakdown for newbies.**

Pros please be more than welcome to add additional facts in the comments.

💵 DXY & Your Money Explained

1️⃣ DXY = dollar’s strength vs other major currencies.
97 ≠ $97. Your $100 is still $100. It’s about global buying power, not face value.

2️⃣ Weak $ → stocks and commodities can appear inflated. Some gains = real growth, some = currency illusion.

3️⃣ Strong $ → multinationals, growth stocks, commodities feel pressure. Domestic/defensive names hold better.

4️⃣ Rule of thumb:
	•	Weak dollar = tailwind for assets
	•	Strong dollar = headwind + volatility

💡 Trader mindset:

“Is this rally real, or just the dollar losing strength?”

Track DXY = understand the oxygen level of markets.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bgO0/little-dollardxy-usd-breakdown*

### Post #1569: 2026-02-01 02:38

**Probability Mindset Vs Certainty Mindset 
“The Switch”**

Humans are hardwired to crave certainty. From childhood, we’re taught to want clear answers, guaranteed outcomes, and “safe” paths. It feels comfortable but in trading, certainty is an illusion.

Markets never guarantee a direction. Any belief that a trade is “sure” sets you up for emotional mistakes, overleveraging, and losses.

Successful traders adopt a probability mindset:
	•	Every trade is a set of possible outcomes, not a prediction.
	•	Risk is calculated, not avoided.
	•	Losses are part of the system, not a personal failure.
	•	You focus on edges and probabilities, not certainties.

Rewiring from certainty to probability is hard because it goes against your conditioning but this is the core of professional trading. Your job isn’t to be right; it’s to manage risk and stack probabilities in your favor over time.

💡 Key takeaway: The market doesn’t care about your confidence. Let go of certainty. Think in probabilities, not guarantees, and your trading results and mindset will change.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bgO9/probability-mindset-vs-certain*

### Post #1575: 2026-02-03 01:37

**Arrogance & Overconfidence Are Expensive in the Market**

One of the most dangerous things in trading isn’t volatility…

It’s certainty.

There are people online who speak about the market like the future already happened.

And the more confident they sound, the more people believe them not because it’s logical, but because certainty feels safe to the human brain.

Most people never learned how to think in probabilities, so they borrow someone else’s confidence instead.

That’s where accounts get blown.

Because notice something…

These “certain” traders almost never talk about:

• What if price does the opposite
• Where they’re wrong
• How much they’re risking
• How they’ll manage the trade if it fails

No contingency plan = emotional trading
Emotional trading = all-in behavior
All-in behavior = account death

Real professionals don’t speak in guarantees.
They speak in scenarios.

“If this holds, I expect continuation.
If it breaks, I’m out.”

That’s not lack of confidence that’s controlled risk.

Arrogance says: “I know.”
Skill says: “I’m prepared either way.”

The market punishes ego and rewards risk management.

If someone sounds 100% sure but has no “what if I’m wrong” plan…

You’re not following a trader.
You’re following a gambler with a microphone.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bgYO/arrogance-and-overconfidence-a*

### Post #1576: 2026-02-03 18:54 — `$SPY`

**🚨 SPY / S&P 500 Failed Bullish Structure = Retail Bull Trap**

$SPY 1D chart Analysis 

What most people see:
“Consolidation near highs = breakout soon.”

What I see:

A failed symmetrical bullish triangle at the top of an extended move this is how distribution looks before downside expansion.


🧠 The Setup

Price has been compressing into a rising symmetrical structure under major resistance. That looks bullish to retail…

But this is actually:

➡️ Late-stage trend
➡️ Buying pressure weakening
➡️ Smart money unloading into strength

When bullish structures fail at highs, they don’t chop…
They unwind hard.


⚠️ The Trigger Level

🔑 675 is the line in the sand

That’s the structure floor.

Once 675 breaks and accepts below, the character shifts from:

“dip buying trend”
to
“sell the rips environment”

That’s where the move down begins.


📉 Downside Path

Initial breakdown targets
	•	667
	•	650
	•	635

From there we likely see a dead cat bounce.


🪤 The Trap Bounce

Expect a bounce back toward 675.

This is where retail thinks:

“See? False breakdown!”

But structurally, this becomes a bearish retest.

If 675 rejects, continuation targets open:

Major downside targets
	•	630
	•	620
	•	605
	•	590
	•	Stretch panic zone: 540

That would be the real unwind of this entire distribution range.


❌ Invalidation (THIS IS IMPORTANT)

A thesis without invalidation is gambling.

This bearish structure is invalid if:
	1.	Price reclaims 675 AND holds above it
	2.	We get acceptance back inside the upper range
	3.	Clean break + hold above the range highs (~695–700 area)

If that happens:
	•	The triangle did NOT fail
	•	It becomes continuation
	•	Shorts are wrong
	•	Market likely pushes to new highs

Until that happens, rallies are suspect.


🧩 What This Really Is

This is not random selling.

This is:
A failed bullish pattern at highs = liquidity event.

These moves are fast because:
	•	Retail buys the “breakout”
	•	Smart money sells into it
	•	Support breaks → trapped longs become sellers

That’s fuel.

Probability > certainty.
We don’t predict. We react to levels.

675 decides if this is just consolidation… or the start of a larger downside cycle.

### Post #1627: 2026-02-08 15:45

**The truth about trading psychology 🧠**

Trading psychology is NOT some mystical mindset game… but it’s also NOT just “have discipline bro.”

Most traders swing to one extreme.

Side A says:
“You have trauma, subconscious blocks, deep identity issues.”

Side B says:
“Just follow your rules and stop being emotional.”

The reality?
Both are incomplete alone.


🔬 Part 1: The Foundation Discipline

Most trading problems are basic human behavior issues showing up under financial pressure.

Revenge trading = poor emotional regulation
Overtrading = impulse control issues
Moving stop losses = lack of rule discipline
Oversizing = ego
Cutting winners early = fear tolerance

The market doesn’t create psychological flaws.
It exposes the ones already there.

This is why structure matters:
• Clear rules
• Defined risk
• Smaller size
• Consistent execution
• Data review

Confidence doesn’t come from mindset work.
It comes from proof.

Psychology improves as a byproduct of disciplined behavior.

That’s the base.

⚜️ Part 2: The Part People Miss Decision Design

Here’s where pure “discipline” thinking falls short:

Under stress, biology beats willpower.

Fatigue
Money pressure
Losing streaks
Big wins
Sleep deprivation

In those moments, “I know better” disappears.

Not because you’re weak.
Because emotion hits before logic.

This is where strategic structure comes in:

You don’t just rely on discipline 
you engineer your environment so bad decisions are harder to make.

Examples:
• Max daily loss that locks you out
• Fixed position sizing rules
• Pre-defined trading windows
• No adding to losers
• Systems of accountability
• Friction before placing impulsive trades

This isn’t mindset fluff.
It’s behavior engineering.

You’re not fixing your mind 
you’re shaping your decision process.

🤝 How It Actually Works Together

Discipline builds the skill.
Structure protects the skill.
Strategy reduces emotional load.

One without the other fails long-term.

If you only focus on mindset → you stay in analysis mode.
If you only rely on willpower → you crack under pressure eventually.

Top performers don’t rely on motivation.

They:
Act with discipline
AND
Build systems so they don’t have to rely on discipline every second.

〰️ The Bottom Line

Trading psychology isn’t mystical.
But it’s not solved by “just try harder” either.

It’s:

Life skills under pressure
+
Systems that make good behavior easier and bad behavior harder

The market rewards people who:
Control themselves
AND
Control their environment.

That’s the real psychology edge.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bhrJ/the-truth-about-trading-psycho*

### Post #1633: 2026-02-09 21:10 — `$SPY`

**$SPY Lessons From The Deep**

🧠 Why I Had a Bullish Bias Today & Took Longs at PMH + 5m Bull FVG

This trade wasn’t random. It was structure, narrative, and confluence all lining up.

📖 The Story of the Market

Premarket sold off hard… but then something changed.

• Selling pressure started slowing
• We formed a double bottom accumulation look
• Price stopped making aggressive new lows

That tells me one thing:
👉 Sellers are getting exhausted.

I’m not looking to short exhaustion I’m looking for the shift.

⚡ The Change of Character

We dipped… then got a strong impulsive move up that broke prior short-term structure.

That displacement = real buying, not just a bounce.

That’s when bias shifts:

Control may be moving from sellers → buyers.

🔑 PMH Flip (The Key Level)

Premarket High (PMH) was resistance earlier.

After the bullish displacement, price pulls back into PMH…

And it holds.

Old resistance → new support.
That’s structural role reversal.

But I still need confirmation.

🟢 5-Min Bull FVG = Confluence

Right inside PMH, we had a 5-minute Bullish Fair Value Gap.

Meaning:
Price moved up so aggressively it left an imbalance and markets often return to rebalance that area before continuing.

So now we’ve stacked:

✔️ PMH support
✔️ Bullish displacement
✔️ 5m Bull FVG
✔️ Higher lows forming

That’s not guessing.
That’s probability stacking.


❌ Why This Was NOT a Short

Most traders see a pullback and panic short.

But this pullback was:
• Controlled
• Into support
• Into imbalance
• Inside bullish structure

That’s not weakness.
That’s a retracement in a developing up-move.

🎯 The Trade Logic

Entry: Reaction inside PMH + Bull FVG
Stop: Below structure / below FVG invalidation
Target: Liquidity above / intraday highs

This is professional trading:

Enter where you’re wrong fast, not where you’re right comfortably.

🧠 The Real Lesson

We don’t buy because candles are green.

We buy when:
Structure shifts ➡️ level holds ➡️ imbalance supports ➡️ risk is defined.

That’s trading with a bias, not emotions. 🥂

Next post is how I spotted the continuation. Stay tuned ✅

### Post #1634: 2026-02-09 21:21 — `$SPY`

**$SPY Pullbacks and Continuations 101**

2/9/2026 

$SPY 5m chart (deep dive journal analysis) 

Most people saw that pullback and thought the move was dead. I was watching for the flip.

Here’s a look into my mind and what actually made this a good long:


📍 The pullback wasn’t weakness

Price dipped into the 50 SMA and a prior demand area.
Selling wasn’t aggressive. No panic candles. No heavy volume dumping.

That’s not breakdown behavior. That’s a controlled pullback in an up move.


🧱 Structure started shifting

That pullback formed an inverse head & shoulders:

• First drop and bounce = left shoulder
• Deeper dip into the 50 SMA = head
• Smaller pullback that held higher = right shoulder

That higher low is where you start paying attention. Sellers couldn’t push it lower anymore.


⚡ The neckline break is the real trigger

When price broke the previous lower-high (the neckline), that’s the key.

That’s the moment structure shifts from:
lower highs & lower lows → higher high potential

That’s a change of character to the upside. Buyers proved they could take control back.

That’s why the long made sense there not at the bottom guess, but after structure confirmed.


🎯 TP wasn’t random

Targeting HOD (High Of Day)

🛑 Stop loss placement = thesis invalidation

Stop goes below the right shoulder low.

If price breaks that:
The higher low fails.
The structure shift fails.
The setup is invalid.

That’s where the idea is wrong so that’s where risk goes.

Big picture 🤳

This wasn’t “pattern trading.” 
This was:

Good location
Structure shift
Confirmation break
Stop at invalidation

### Post #1649: 2026-02-13 03:43

**How To Deploy Cash (Long Term / LEAPS)**

Most retail deploys cash at the worst time.

After the breakout.
After the hype.
After the move already happened.

By then, risk is elevated and R/R is trash.

That’s reaction not positioning.

1️⃣ Direction First. Always.

Before deploying size ask:

• Are we printing higher highs / higher lows on weekly?
• Is structure intact?
• Is macro supportive?
• Are we above key HTF averages?

If direction isn’t clear you wait.

Cash is a position.

2️⃣ Buy Fear. Not Euphoria.

You deploy on:

• Pullbacks into demand
• Prior resistance turning support
• Key fib retracements
• Volatility spikes
• Sentiment resets

You don’t deploy after a vertical 6% candle and rocket emojis.

Retail buys strength.
Professionals buy retracements.


3️⃣ Scale In Never Full Port

Build positions in tranches.

Example:
• 25% first support
• 25% deeper demand
• 25% confirmation reclaim
• 25% reserve for flush

This removes emotion and improves cost basis.


4️⃣ Let Liquidity Do the Work

Best long-term entries usually happen:

• After forced selling
• After macro fear
• After weak hands get cleaned out
• When volatility expands

Discomfort = opportunity.

Comfort = late.

5️⃣ Define Invalidation

Even long-term capital needs structure.

If weekly structure breaks
If macro shifts
If trend fails

You reassess. You don’t pray.

We don’t deploy when retail gets loud.
We deploy when structure aligns and emotion is high.

Patience builds wealth.
Impulse funds someone else’s exit.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bhL8/how-to-deploy-cash-long-term-l*

### Post #1652: 2026-02-13 20:24

**Friday Recap TA 101**


	Today was a textbook example of letting price come to you instead of forcing trades.

1️⃣ Context
	•	We came in with prior weakness and compression.
	•	Early structure formed a falling wedge = relief bounce scenario.
	•	Key level overhead: $685.90–$686 (clear rejection zone).

2️⃣ The Chop
Morning session was pure compression.
When price coils like that between trendlines, that’s not the time to size up that’s the time to wait.

Chop is expensive if you’re impatient.

3️⃣ The Long Setup
Entry came on:
	•	Reclaim of previous range support
	•	Breakout from wedge compression
	•	Momentum shift + structure break

Target was into the $685.90 supply zone.

That level wasn’t random it was prior rejection. Logical TP for calls.

4️⃣ The Transition
At $685.90:
	•	Prior sellers stepped in again
	•	Clear rejection
	•	Rising wedge formed into resistance

That’s where you:
	•	Take profits on calls
	•	Look for puts on confirmation/rejection

5️⃣ What This Teaches
	•	Trade level to level.
	•	Pay yourself into supply.
	•	Don’t marry bias.
	•	Compression leads to expansion.
	•	The same level can be TP for longs and entry for shorts.

Invalidation below $680 opens the door for further downside expansion.
Acceptance above $686 flips that zone to support and targets higher.

Disciplined execution. No guessing. Just reacting to structure. 👑
