### Post #1656: 2026-02-14 14:55

**Time to share my Strategy 😆**

The "Big Picture" Strategy (Happy VDay from me 2 u here’s ur gift)

Trading isn't about predicting the future; it's about probability. By using these four indicators, im looking for a "confluence" a moment where the trend, the price level, and the volume all agree on the same direction.

1. The MA Ribbon: Your "GPS" 🗺️
Think of Moving Averages (MAs) as the road markers on a highway. They tell you which way the traffic is flowing so you don’t drive into oncoming traffic.


💚 5 & 20 EMA (The Micro & Short-Term): These are "fast" lines. They follow the price closely. If the 5 is above the 20, the immediate momentum is bullish (up).
💛 50 SMA (The Mid-Term): This is the "Trend Decider." Many institutional traders look at this. If price is above it, we generally look for buys.
❤️ 200 EMA (The Long-Term): This is the "Anchor." It tells you the overall health of the market. Why it works: When all these lines are fanned out in order (5 > 20 > 50 > 200), it shows a strong, healthy trend. If they are tangled together, the market is "sideways" and dangerous to trade. 

2. 4x HTF H/L: The "Floor and Ceiling"

Price doesn't move in a straight line; it moves from one level to the next. The Highs and Lows of the 1-hour, 4-hour, Daily, and Weekly timeframes are like invisible walls.

➡️ Higher Timeframes (HTF) Rule: A Weekly High is much "stronger" than a 1-hour High.

➡️The Strategy: We use these levels to know where to take profits or where the price might bounce. If the price is approaching a "Daily High," we expect resistance.
 Why it works: These are the levels where the "big money" (banks and institutions) have placed their orders. 

3. Volume & RSI: The "Fuel Gauge"

If the MA Ribbon tells us the direction and the HTF levels tell us where to stop, Volume and RSI tell us if the move has any power.

📊Volume: This is the amount of money trading. A price breakout with low volume is often a "fake-out." A breakout with high volume is a "breakaway."

💪RSI (Relative Strength Index): I usually look for divergences. If the Price makes a lower low and RSI makes a higher high. That’s a bullish divergence Vice versa for bearish divergences

How it all comes together ⚙️

To be a successful trader, you need to combine these with Price Action (how the candles look) and Market Structure(Higher Highs and Higher Lows).
1. Check the Ribbon: Is the trend up or down? (Don't fight the 200 EMA).
2. Identify the Levels: Where is the nearest 4-hour or Daily High/Low?
3. Wait for Price Action: Does the price "react" at one of those levels (example: a bullish engulfing candle)?
4. Confirm with Volume and RSI. Is the "fuel" there to support the move? Is the RSI following price or is there a build up in momentum waiting for expansion?

Why "Simple" is Better

New traders often clutter their charts with 10+ indicators. This leads to "Analysis Paralysis" where one indicator says "Buy" and another says "Sell," so you do nothing. By keeping it to these 3 + the RSI, you have a clear, repeatable "Yes/No" checklist for every trade.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bhRz/time-to-share-my-strategy*

### Post #1684: 2026-02-19 18:08

**TA 101**

Price doesn’t move because you feel something.
It moves because participants defend or lose key levels.

Learn structure.
Learn narrative.
Then let price do the work.

Emotion guesses.
Structure confirms.

This chart is a perfect example of why technical narrative and basic market structure matter.

What most emotional traders see:
	•	“Price looks cheap here”
	•	“It already dropped, it HAS to bounce”
	•	“I feel like this is the bottom”

So they buy… blindly.

What the chart actually showed:
	•	Lower highs + lower lows → bearish structure
	•	Price failed to reclaim prior support (now resistance)
	•	Rejection at a previous low = confirmation, not a guess
	•	Breakdown below structure = BOS (Break of Structure)

That’s not emotion.
That’s information.

Why the short made sense:
	•	Entry was based on previous low rejection
	•	Stop defined by structure (not hope)
	•	Target aligned with the next liquidity zone
	•	Trade played out because the market followed its narrative

No prediction.
No “I think.”
Just reaction to what price proved.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bh9L/ta-101*

### Post #1702: 2026-02-25 01:12 — `$PYPL`

**If Stripe bought PayPal would $PYPL still trade?**

Short answer: Usually no.

If PayPal were fully acquired by Stripe, Inc. PYPL ticker would typically be delisted once the deal closes.

What normally happens:
	•	Shareholders receive cash, stock of the buyer, or a mix
	•	PYPL stops trading after the acquisition is finalized
	•	Your broker automatically converts your shares based on the deal terms

The only time PYPL would keep trading is if PayPal remained a separate public entity which is rare in full buyouts.

🧠 Key takeaway:
When a public company is fully acquired, the ticker usually disappears value transfers to the new structure.

This is why we must focus on the deal structure, not just headlines. 💯


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/biws/if-stripe-bought-paypal-would-*

### Post #1712: 2026-03-02 19:45 `[Chart]`

**Can you recognize patterns?**

SPY 1D chart 📊 

Price kept reacting to the same demand zone around 678–680.

You had multiple prior taps into that blue box with strong support/reactions (those circled wicks). That’s repeated evidence of buyers stepping in at the same level. When price revisits a level that has already proven to hold multiple times, that’s not guessing that’s pattern recognition.

So when SPY opened right back into that demand area again It was all about structure. Everything else was noise. 

Demand had already shown:
	•	Strong rejection of downside continuation
	•	Aggressive buying response (reaction)
	•	Consistent defense of the level ($678-680)

Calls off the demand zone made sense because:
	1.	The level was clearly defined.
	2.	It had multiple prior reactions.
	3.	Risk was tight (below the zone).
	4.	Upside liquidity sat back toward 690–697 resistance.

That’s just market memory repeating itself. 🧠🧩

Technical analysis doesn’t care about noise 🗣️

Price respects order flow and structure. When a level has proven buyers are there, you trade the reaction not the headlines.  ✅

Plain old pattern recognition. 🥂

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/biXC/can-you-recognize-patterns*

### Post #1722: 2026-03-06 04:18 — `$SPY`

**Fun fact**

When the price action makes a lower low but the RSI holds a higher/equal low this generally indicates momentum compression. 

Since price made a lower low and rsi a higher/equal low the momentum compression In this case is bullish. 

The opposite of that is price action making a higher high and RSI making a lower/equal high which is bearish. 

Food for thought 🥂

$SPY looks good above $680+ 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bje4/fun-fact*

### Post #1737: 2026-03-11 03:53

**Paytience**

Trading is a strange psychological transformation.

At first, you chase money. Every candle feels like opportunity slipping away, every move you miss feels personal. You think progress comes from doing more more trades, more predictions, more control.

But real growth begins the moment you slow down.

You realize the market isn’t something you conquer, it’s something you wait for. The edge appears in patience. Money stops being something you chase and becomes something that comes to you when conditions align. Mistakes stop feeling like failures and start becoming teachers. Every loss sharpens perception. Every missed trade strengthens discipline. Over time, you stop reacting and start observing.

And that’s when the shift happens.

The market begins rewarding the trader who can sit still, think clearly, and act only when the moment is undeniable. Because in trading, progress isn’t measured by how often you trade 

it’s measured by how well you learn to wait. 💯

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bjz8/paytience*

📊 BTC Weekly Setup Hidden Strength in Compression  

We’re seeing a key shift in structure + momentum right now on Bitcoin.

➡️ Momentum fully reset  
➡️ Structure held strong  

This isn’t just a bounce… this is continuation behavior.

Combined with the falling wedge, we now have:
• Compression tightening  
• Sellers losing momentum  
• Buyers stepping in earlier  

This type of setup typically leads to expansion once broken.

❌ Invalidation:

### Post #1830: 2026-03-31 03:11

**Advice**

Doing too much in the market is the fastest way to stay stuck.

Every tick doesn’t need a reaction. Every candle isn’t a signal. Every move isn’t yours to catch.

Human psychology isn’t built to process constant action it’s built to protect you. When you overtrade, over-leverage, and react to every little move, you’re not trading… you’re feeding emotion.

Discipline isn’t doing more.  
It’s doing LESS, but with intention.

The real edge?
Waiting. Letting price come to YOU.  
Letting confirmations build.  
Letting invalidations be clear.

Most people lose not because they’re wrong…  
but because they won’t sit still long enough to be right.

Slow down. Be selective.  
Precision over activity every time.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bk1D/advice*

### Post #1867: 2026-04-13 20:28

**To everyone who thinks my TA is just a fancy way of saying “up or down”**

You’re exposing your level, not mine.

Anyone can guess direction. That’s gambling.
What I do is map probability, structure, timing, and reaction zones before price even gets there.

I don’t say “up.”
I say:
– where it goes up from  
– why it goes up  
– what confirms it  
– where I’m wrong  

That’s the difference between prediction and execution.

If all you hear is “up or down,” you’re not ready yet and that’s okay.

But don’t confuse your lack of understanding with lack of depth.

There’s layers to this game.
Either learn them… or keep calling it luck while others get paid.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bl0E/to-everyone-who-thinks-my-ta-i*

### Post #1889: 2026-04-18 11:24

**Your best trade was a mistake**

FOMO trades that work build bad habits,
Impulsive revenge trading that got you back in the green? Bad habit. 

Made 1000x gain because someone told you but you have no idea how you got there in the first place. Bad habit.


The market rewards bad habits to later trap and consume you through the same “habit” that saved you once. 

Trade with discipline and patience. Made a gain following your rules step by step with discipline and patience? Good habit good trade. Lost money doing the same thing. Still a good trade. 

Not every trade is a winner. That’s apart of the game. But throwing away a good system and the right discipline just to make money will cost you more in the long run. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bmrD/your-best-trade-was-a-mistake*

### Post #1891: 2026-04-18 17:10

**Words every trader should rebuke**

language which drives mindset which controls execution.

❌ BANNED WORDS (Kill these immediately)
	•	“I think…”
	•	“Maybe…”
	•	“It has to…”
	•	“It can’t go higher/lower…”
	•	“I hope…”
	•	“I want…”
	•	“It should bounce…”
	•	“I feel like…”
	•	“I missed it…”
	•	“I knew it…”
	•	“Just this one trade…”
	•	“Let me make it back…”
	•	“It’ll come back…”
	•	“I’ll move my stop…”


⚠️ THE ONES THAT SOUND SMART (but will still cook you)
	•	“It’s extended…”
	•	“It’s due for a pullback…”
	•	“That’s a lot of volume…”
	•	“Everyone’s bullish/bearish…”
	•	“News will push it…”


✅ REPLACE WITH OPERATOR LANGUAGE
	•	“If price holds ___ → then I look for ___”
	•	“Break/reclaim of ___ = trigger”
	•	“Invalidation = ___ (no exceptions)”
	•	“Risk defined before entry”
	•	“No setup = no trade”
	•	“React > predict”
	•	“Probabilities, not certainties”


🧠 REALITY CHECK

The market doesn’t care what you think.
It rewards discipline, not opinions.

The second you say:
hope
should
has to

You’ve already stepped out of your system. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bmse/words-every-trader-should-rebu*

### Post #1912: 2026-04-30 21:04

**Lack of Discipline Is a form of self devaluation.**

You have a plan. You don’t follow it.

You have rules and you find yourself breaking them when they matter most. That’s not a knowledge gap. That’s a self-respect gap.

Moving your stop loss isn’t risk management. It’s you negotiating with your own rules and losing. The version of you that set that stop was thinking clearly, before emotion was involved. When you move it, you’re telling yourself your own plan isn’t worth following. That’s self-disrespect in real time, with real money.

Revenge trading is self-punishment disguised as aggression. You’re not attacking the market after that loss you’re attacking yourself. No setup, no confirmation, sizing up out of anger. The market doesn’t care about your feelings. It will take that second position just as fast as the first. Now you’re down double with your psychology completely wrecked.

Overtrading is the quiet one. No setup, no edge, just needing to be in something. Your dopamine system gets hooked on the action instead of the process. Most professional traders spend 90% of their time doing nothing waiting for the right moment. More trades does not equal more effort. It equals more exposure to a market you have no edge in right now.

Heres the catch

When you respect yourself, you keep promises to yourself. You honor your own commitments the same way you would for someone else. The trader who moves stops, revenge trades, and overtrades is saying unconsciously 

“my rules don’t matter. I don’t matter.”

The market is a mirror. It will show you exactly how much you value yourself, in real time, with real money.

Protect your capital. Protect your mind. They’re the same thing.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bniL/lack-of-discipline-is-a-form-o*

### Post #1932: 2026-05-06 15:15

**Advice**

One of the biggest levels in trading isn’t on the chart.
It’s the level of self-respect you have for your own rules. Today I told myself I would NOT trade unless price broke above the supply zone (purple box) and out of the chop for a confirmed move.
So I waited.

No forcing entries.
No boredom trades.
No “maybe it’ll run.”
Just patience until conditions were met.

Then the breakout came.
I executed exactly how I planned, took my money and got out.

Could I have made more? Sure.
But that’s not the point.

The point is I respected myself enough to follow my own conditions instead of betraying them for dopamine.

Most traders don’t lose because they lack analysis.
They lose because they abandon themselves mid-trade.

Discipline is keeping promises to yourself when emotions try to renegotiate them.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bnGR/advice*

### Post #1942: 2026-05-09 04:35

**Everyone becomes an investor at the top**

If you’re new to the market READ THIS!

Semis at all time highs.  
SPY at all time highs.  
Media headlines screaming AI revolution.  
Your barber, coworker, and cousin suddenly opening brokerages after a 100%+ move already happened.

This is how bag holders are created.

People don’t study market cycles.  
They don’t learn risk management.  
They don’t understand valuation, positioning, liquidity, or psychology.

They buy emotion.  
They buy headlines.  
They buy euphoria.

Then the first real correction hits and suddenly:
- “The market is manipulated”
- “I’m long term now”
- “I sold for a loss”

No. You entered without a plan.

The market transfers money from emotional participants to disciplined ones.

Stop thinking:
“it’s going up so I need to buy now.”

Start thinking:
“where is risk lowest and reward highest?”

The best opportunities usually feel uncomfortable not obvious enough to be on every news headline and TikTok feed.

By the time the media convinces the public, smart money has usually been positioned for months.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bnUZ/everyone-becomes-an-investor-a*

### Post #1944: 2026-05-09 04:54

**A composed mindset at All time highs**

A composed mindset comes from removing urgency and attachment from your decisions.

Most traders are emotionally unstable because they subconsciously believe:
“This trade NEEDS to work.”
“If I don’t get in now I’ll miss out!” 

That creates fear, hesitation, revenge trading, overtrading, and emotional decision making.

A few practical things that helped me:

- Stop sizing so big that your PnL controls your emotions
- Trade less, observe more
- Wait for YOUR conditions, not movement
- Accept beforehand that any trade can fail
- Focus on process > money
- Journal emotional mistakes, not just technical ones
- Don’t stare at every candle tick once in position
- Understand that patience is also a position

The market rewards people who can remain objective while everyone else becomes emotional.

Composure is built through repetition, discipline, and self awareness not motivation.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bnU1/a-composed-mindset-at-all-time*

### Post #1947: 2026-05-09 05:22

**RSI Divergence**

RSI divergence is when price and momentum stop agreeing.

Price makes a new high/low, but RSI doesn’t.
That usually means the move is losing strength and could reverse soon.

Think of it like this. A car can still be moving forward, but if the engine is losing power, eventually the car slows down or turns around.

That’s what RSI divergence tries to show.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bnU7/rsi-divergence*

### Post #1950: 2026-05-09 22:42

**PSA**

Some people confuse chart analysts, educators, and traders with financial advisors managing client money. Those are completely different things.

I don’t manage portfolios.
I don’t force investments.
I don’t tell people to blindly buy stocks.

What I do is share technical analysis, market structure, probabilities, risk management, and my personal view on what the charts are showing in real time. If I find a stock attractive, that does NOT mean “this stock is guaranteed to go up.” It means the setup, momentum, liquidity, structure, sector strength, and risk/reward align with my thesis based on the data I’m analyzing.

That’s the entire point of trading and investing:

- gathering information
- forming a thesis
- managing risk
- adapting if wrong

No real trader has certainty.
Only probabilities.

The market doesn’t reward people for blindly following others. It rewards people who learn how to think independently, manage emotions, and understand risk. That’s why I constantly tell people

Do your own research.
Challenge ideas.
Ask questions.
Learn the process.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bnWK/psa*

---

### Post #1951: 2026-05-09 22:58

**Technical analysis + Charts (PSA 2)**

Fear, greed, earnings, rates, AI hype, sector rotation, liquidity, institutions buying and selling all eventually get reflected through price action. That’s why technical analysis matters. A chart helps identify where buyers and sellers are active where momentum shifts and where risk becomes invalid. TA isn’t the entire puzzle but it’s a very important piece of it. (Always consider all factors {do your own research}) 

Macro is the why.
Fundamentals build the story.
TA helps with timing and navigation.

Charts don’t provide certainty they provide structure in an uncertain market.

For anyone new finding my page on AfterHour, my purpose here isn’t to sell dreams or hand out random lottery plays. My goal is to teach people how to think through the market using probabilities, confirmations, invalidations and risk management.

Anyone can get lucky once. Longevity comes from discipline, timing, emotional control and adapting to changing market conditions.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bnWN/technical-analysis-charts-psa-*

### Post #1964: 2026-05-12 04:20

**AAR - By TrynaMakeIt**

Acknowledge, Address, Reform
For when you burn out 

	•	Acknowledge
Accept what happened without judgment. The loss, the mistake, the emotion it already happened. No excuses, no self-hate, just honesty.

	•	Address
Identify why it happened. Was it sizing, patience, FOMO, discipline, environment, or mindset? One or two real causes not a story.

	•	Reform
Make a clear, specific adjustment for the next trade/session. Smaller size, fewer trades, stricter entry, step away, or no trade at all.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bn4T/aar-by-trynamakeit*

### Post #2006: 2026-05-20 04:17

**“Trust your instincts” sounds cool until real money is involved.**

Instincts and vibes are NOT a strategy.
Most people hiding behind that phrase simply don’t have a repeatable system, risk model, or edge to explain what they’re doing.

A million dollar portfolio doesn’t mean shit if there’s no structure behind it. You can size into bad decisions too. Money can amplify luck just as fast as it amplifies incompetence.

Real traders operate with:

* confirmations
* invalidation levels
* risk management
* probabilities
* data
* discipline

Not “I had a feeling.”

Anybody can look like a genius in a bull run. The market eventually exposes whether you had a process or just dopamine and confidence.

There’s a huge difference between developed intuition built from years of structured experience… and blindly freestyling while calling it instinct.

(CONSTRUCTIVE CRITICISM NOT HATING LOL JUST USING MY FREEDOM OF SPEECH TO SHARE A LESSON IN LOSS) 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/boHQ/trust-your-instincts-sounds-co*

### Post #2009: 2026-05-20 20:05 — `$NVDA`

**$NVDA**

Daily candle at close is a Doji

A Doji is a single candlestick pattern where an asset's opening and closing prices are practically identical, creating a cross or plus sign. It indicates market indecision, signaling that a previous trend may be weakening and a potential price reversal or consolidation could follow

In short term, indecision/weakness let’s see where we are tomorrow

**Trading Psychology 🔑**

A lot of new traders come into the market obsessed with entries and take profits. “Where do I buy?” “Where do I sell?” “What contract?” 

But the uncomfortable truth is this:

Your psychology is what decides whether those entries even matter.

Two traders can take the exact same setup and get completely different results because one panics at drawdown, cuts winners too early, revenge trades losses, or sizes too big emotionally.

Trading psychology isn’t some motivational buzzword. It’s your ability to stay composed when money is moving against you, your ability to WAIT when conditions aren’t there, and your ability to follow a plan even when emotions are screaming louder than logic.

Most beginners think trading is about finding the holy grail entry.
Experienced traders know survival and consistency come from behavior.

A bad mindset will destroy a good setup.
A disciplined mindset can survive imperfect setups.

The market exposes who you really are:
- impatient people force trades
- emotional people overtrade
- greedy people hold too long
- fearful people sell too early
- undisciplined people ignore invalidation

That’s why psychology and risk management come before profits.

Anyone can click buy.
Very few people can manage themselves.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bo2z/trading-psychology*

### Post #2060: 2026-05-27 18:04

**“Government injecting liquidity” has become massive FOMO bait**

Just to clear up the idea, people hear “liquidity injection and instantly think stocks can only go up, no pullbacks. It is true that liquidity can help push markets higher because more money flows through the system… but that alone is NOT a trading strategy. 

People blindly buying headlines without confirmation are the same people that get trapped at local tops when the market pulls back 2 days later. 

Liquidity is just one piece of the puzzle. Price action, structure, trend, volume, confirmations, and risk management still matter way more than a bullish headline on Twitter.

Stay woke folks 🥂

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bpfA/government-injecting-liquidity*

### Post #2212: 2026-06-09 02:09

**The News is Trading You, Not the Market**

A lot of traders get trapped refreshing headlines, doom-scrolling X, and trying to predict how every geopolitical event, tariff, meeting, or news release will affect the market.

Meanwhile, the chart is literally showing you what the market is doing in real time.

I don’t trade fear. I don’t trade headlines. I trade price.

The market doesn’t care about your opinion on the news. It cares about buyers, sellers, support, resistance, momentum, and liquidity.

If you’re constantly changing your bias because of every headline that pops up, you’re letting the news trade you instead of trading the market.

Take it one day at a time. Mark your levels. Wait for confirmation. Follow your plan.

The chart is the final vote.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bqsD/the-news-is-trading-you-not-th*

### Post #2281: 2026-06-26 17:18

**Introducing The Big Picture Indicator**

This one has been a long time coming.

For years I’ve been refining how I read the tape bias, structure, momentum, volume, higher-timeframe context  and translating that into the setups I share with you every day. Now for the first time, that entire framework lives inside a single indicator you can put on your own chart.

Meet Big Picture Unified (BPS-U). The official indicator of The Kingdom.

Not a signal service. Not an arrow that tells you when to buy. It’s a complete market-state engine the same way I actually read the tape, coded into a tool that works for you in real time.

The dashboard reads:

🟢 Bias (Strong Bull / Mixed / Strong Bear)

🟢 Higher-timeframe confirmation

🟢 Momentum (RSI-based)

🟢 Volume flow (buying vs selling pressure)

🟢 Structure (HH/HL or LH/LL)

🟢 Extension from 20 EMA

🟢 Session timing and opening lockout

The trade section gives you:

⚡ Star-rated confidence score (★ to ★★★)

⚡ Position size guidance (Full / Half / Quarter)

⚡ Locked Entry, Stop, T1, T2 levels in R multiples

⚡ Live R:R display

⚡ Last 5 trade results tracker

⚡ PLAY row — one line that tells you exactly what to do right now

Built-in protection includes:

🛡 Failed-break filter (no more 1-tick fakeouts)

🛡 Double bottom/top detector

🛡 HTF hard-gate (won’t short into a bullish HTF)

🛡 Chop-immune breakdown entry for real structural breaks

🛡 News spike lockout

🛡 Opening bell lockout

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

WHO IT’S FOR

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Beginner Mode is ON by default clean chart, only the highest-conviction setups. As you learn, flip it off to unlock the full SMC framework.

Works on a FREE TradingView account. No paid TV plan needed.

Best on intraday timeframes (1m–15m) for SPY, QQQ, and liquid stocks.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

HOW TO GET ACCESS

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Join The Big Picture Indicator tier ($15/month) https://www.patreon.com/TrynaMakeIt?utm_campaign=creatorshare_creator and DM me on Discord or Patreon at trynamakeit_1 with your TradingView username. I’ll add you to the invite-only script manually.

this tier grants exclusive access to the indicator ALONE not including The Kingdom Discord  access which on its own also grants indicator access + community access.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

I built this because I wanted something I could put in front of a new trader and have it actually teach them how to read the market  not just hand them signals they don’t understand.

If you’ve been watching my analysis and wondering how I see what I see, this is the answer.

Welcome to the next level. 👑

Educational tool only. Not financial advice. Not a signal service. No indicator wins every trade. Always use a stop, manage your risk, and trade your own plan. By accessing this tool you accept full responsibility for any result.

### Post #2353: 2026-07-11 00:00

**Four screenshots, three days, one lesson I keep relearning**


Been journaling my QQQ analysis all week and looking back at the timestamps taught me more than the trade did.

July 7, 9pm: QQQ closing at $709, sitting right on wedge support. I didn’t write a prediction. I wrote two sentences: bounce off $705 + reclaim $710 = buyers take it back toward $730. Break below $705 with rejection = door opens to $686. That’s it. Two branches, both mapped.

July 9, midnight: $705 wicked and held. Updated the chart $710 now the level that matters, eyeing $715–$720, and I wrote “invalid below $705” in red so future me couldn’t rationalize holding a broken thesis. Also caught RSI making higher lows while price went sideways. Quiet strength.

July 9, evening: $723. Through the whole target zone.

July 10 close: $725.51, knocking on the $730 trigger. Next branch already written.

The thing I keep relearning: the July 7 chart would have been correct either way. If $705 broke, the plan was right. It held, the plan was right. Because the plan was never “QQQ goes up.” The plan was “here’s what each outcome means and here’s what I do in each case.”

Early on I thought TA was about being smart enough to know the direction. Now I think it’s about being humble enough to write down what proves you wrong before you’re wrong.

The red “invalid below $705” text is the most important thing on any of these charts. Everything else is just decoration around an exit plan.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bsv2/four-screenshots-three-days-on*

### Post #2355: 2026-07-11 01:48

**The bearish signal I almost mistraded (and the trade hiding inside it)**

Alright AfterHour fam here goes nothing…….. 

July 7, I’m looking at SPY around $746 and there’s a textbook bear RSI divergence on the 4h price grinding out equal highs while momentum makes lower highs. Early me would’ve shorted that on sight, full size, no conditions.

Instead I wrote down triggers. $745 last support. Breakdown below opens $743, then 740–738. Bear zone 728–715 if $740 goes. And on the other side, one sentence that ended up mattering most: $750+ = bullish breakout, divergence or not.

July 8 into the 9th: the $745 breakdown actually came the exact thing I was waiting for. Took shorts per the plan, rode it down toward $740, exited at the level. Not because I felt like it. Because $740 was written on the chart before the trade existed, and my plan said that’s where the thesis was spent.

Then the part that used to be impossible for me: I waited.

$740 held. A bull flag started forming off that low and underneath it, a bull divergence on the bounce. Momentum turning up while price sat on support. Same tool that warned me at the highs was now whispering at the lows. The flag broke, price reclaimed $745–$750, and that became the long setup short thesis completed, closed, and reversed with structure instead of stubbornness.

July 10: SPY closes $754.81. Clean $750 breakout, targets shift to $753, $755, $760, and $750 flips to support.

Here’s what I keep chewing on: both trades came from the same chart. The divergence at the top didn’t make me short the $745 break did. The bounce at $740 didn’t make me long the flag breakout and reclaim did. Signals told me where to look. Levels told me when to act.

Old me would’ve shorted the divergence early, gotten shaken out, then watched the real move from the sidelines out of spite. Current me just followed the sentences I wrote on July 7.

Most of my edge isn’t in seeing signals. It’s in the boring line I repeat to myself before entering “and here’s what has to happen first.” 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bsv6/the-bearish-signal-i-almost-mi*

### Post #2364: 2026-07-14 15:59 — `$SPY`

**PSA**

Invalid below $…… does not mean 

Thesis invalidates if price breaks below a zone.

It means thesis invalidates if price breaks and closes below the zone. 

Don’t forget how the market works.

Just because a zone hits doesn’t mean price has to stay there. 

The close is key 🔑


Example: $SPY wicked down to $748 today.

But did it close below $748? No.

$750 support regained on the next candle close and the upside thesis immediately remains valid.

### Post #2375: 2026-07-16 00:28 — `$ASTS`

**$ASTS pt 2**

ASTS Bull Case After the Private Offering 🚀🛰️

A lot of people are focused on the dilution fears, but here’s the bull case:

✅ ASTS raised $1 billion before they were desperate for cash.

✅ The notes carry a low interest rate and convert at a price significantly above where the stock was trading, which is far better than a traditional discounted share offering.

✅ This removes a major funding overhang. Building a space-based cellular network isn’t cheap, and capital was one of the biggest concerns for investors.

✅ More capital means more satellites, faster deployment, and a potentially quicker path to commercial revenue.

✅ Institutional investors were willing to commit capital for the long term, which can be viewed as a vote of confidence in the company’s future.

The market often reacts negatively to convertible offerings in the short term because of dilution concerns. However, if management uses this capital effectively, the long-term story may actually be stronger today than it was before the offering.

Short-term pain. Long-term execution. That’s the bet.

### Post #2417: 2026-07-24 00:11

**Message to traders during uncertainty**

One of the biggest mistakes traders make is marrying a bull or bear narrative.

If you’re a day trader, today’s bias doesn’t owe you anything tomorrow. The market doesn’t care what you wanted it to do or what it did yesterday.

Stay fluid.

Approach each trading day with a fresh mindset. Build a bias based on what the market is showing you today, but be willing to abandon it the moment price action proves you wrong.

Nobody truly knows what the market is capable of doing next. We can analyze, prepare, and stack probabilities in our favor but certainty doesn’t exist.

That’s why preparation matters more than prediction.

Have your levels marked. Know your invalidation. Plan for both bullish and bearish scenarios before the opening bell. When the market tips its hand, execute the plan instead of forcing your opinion.

Preparation creates confidence because you’re ready for multiple outcomes, not just the one you hope happens.

Trade probabilities, not predictions.

Day by day is the way.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bs7O/message-to-traders-during-unce*

### Post #2423: 2026-07-27 02:50 — `$SPY, $QQQ`

**$SPY VS $QQQ**

One thing that’s really stood out lately is the difference between the S&P 500 (SPY) and the Nasdaq 100 (QQQ). A lot of people assume they move together, but they don’t always. The reason is diversification. The Nasdaq 100 is heavily concentrated in technology. When the mega-cap tech names get hit, QQQ feels it almost immediately because so much of its weight is tied to those companies. The S&P 500, on the other hand, has exposure across multiple sectors technology, healthcare, financials, industrials, consumer staples, energy, utilities, and more. That diversification creates resilience. Right now we’re seeing sector rotation. Money isn’t necessarily leaving the market altogether it’s simply moving from one area to another. While parts of tech have cooled off, sectors like healthcare have attracted buying interest, helping offset some of the weakness elsewhere.

That’s why SPY has been holding up better than QQQ. It’s not that the market is magically stronger it’s that capital is rotating into sectors that carry meaningful weight inside the S&P 500. This is a great reminder that the market is constantly reallocating capital. One sector can be weak while another quietly carries the index.

As traders, we don’t want to marry a bullish or bearish narrative. We want to follow where the money is flowing. Sector rotation is one of the clearest examples of why staying flexible matters.
Trade what’s happening not what you think should happen. ♥️💎

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/btdo/dollarspy-vs-dollarqqq*

### Post #2505: 2026-08-05 01:34

**Price Discovery: Where New Trends Are Born**

One of the most important concepts every trader should understand is price discovery.

Price discovery happens when an asset trades into territory where there isn’t much recent price history to lean on. Instead of reacting to old support and resistance levels, the market has to discover what buyers and sellers believe is a fair price.

This is why all-time highs can feel “easy” to trade one day and incredibly volatile the next. The market is literally writing a new chapter.

Now: How to Navigate Price Discovery?

When there are no obvious resistance levels overhead, psychology becomes one of your biggest tools. Humans naturally think in round numbers. Institutions know this, algorithms know this, and retail traders certainly know this.

For example, if SPY breaks above $750 into price discovery, the next areas I pay attention to aren’t random they’re psychological levels:

- $750
- $755
- $760
- $765
* $770

These prices often act as temporary magnets where traders:

Take profits
Scale into positions
Place stop losses
Trigger algorithmic buying or selling

Because thousands of participants are watching the same numbers, they frequently become self-fulfilling support or resistance levels.

Turning Resistance Into Support

One of the strongest confirmations during price discovery is seeing an old psychological resistance become new support.

Example:

SPY breaks above $755
It pulls back to test $750
Buyers defend the level
Price continues toward $760

That simple retest tells you demand is still present and that buyers are willing to defend higher prices.

The process then repeats:

$760 → $765 → $770 → $775…

Every successful breakout creates another opportunity for the previous resistance to become support.

Don’t Fight Price Discovery

Many traders make the mistake of constantly trying to short new highs simply because “price has gone too far.”

But markets don’t care how expensive something feels.

Strong trends can remain irrational much longer than traders expect.

Instead of guessing tops, let price tell the story. Watch how it behaves around psychological levels, wait for confirmation, and trade what you see not what you think should happen.

In trading, price discovery isn’t about predicting the future it’s about following the footprints institutions leave behind as they discover the next fair value.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/btHl/price-discovery-where-new-tren*

### Post #2521: 2026-08-06 22:06

**Learn to walk away**

If you got chopped up trading after 12pm est you’re in middle of a valuable lesson. The market pays in bursts and spends the rest of the day trying to take it back. That afternoon chop isn’t opportunity it’s statistically built to bleed you. Small loss, small loss, revenge size, big loss. Forcing trades through it out of boredom isn’t discipline. It’s the amateur move that turns a green day red one 50-cent scalp at a time. Longevity isn’t screen time. It’s recognizing when your zone is done and ending the trading day.

In the image below you can see clearly where market favored the upside and where it turned over to favor the downside. After that. Dry volume. Take the leg. Walk away. Protect the win.

If you trade with us in the Kingdom there’s a reason we end it at 12

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/btPi/learn-to-walk-away*

### Post #2556: 2026-08-18 02:45

**Technical analysis 101**

The Broadening Ascending Wedge 

Today’s breakdown consisted of an ascending broadening wedge which is a bearish chart pattern that forms during an uptrend, resembling an upward-sloping megaphone. It features diverging trendlines connecting higher highs and higher lows, driven by rising market volatility and indecision before a downward breakout.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bus9/technical-analysis-101*

### Post #2623: 2026-08-31 02:03 — `$BTC`

**$BTC PSA**

IF THE RECENT BITCOIN POP INDUCED FOMO IN YOU, READ THIS.

You did NOT “miss the pump.”

That is FOMO language and it’s exactly how retail gets convinced to buy the top.

Markets don’t move in a straight line. There will always be pullbacks, consolidations, and opportunities to enter.

The goal isn’t to chase green candles because you’re afraid you’re late.

Learn to read the pullbacks.

The best opportunities often come when retail is scared, running for the exits, calling for a crash, and selling their positions.

While everyone else is asking:

“Is Bitcoin crashing?”

You should be asking:

“Is this pullback giving me an opportunity?”

Don’t let FOMO dictate your entries.

📈 You don’t have to catch the pump. You have to learn how to catch the setup.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bviA/dollarbtc-psa*

### Post #2644: 2026-09-01 21:27

**PSA**

Receipts don’t mean shit by themselves. 

A screenshot of a green P&L doesn’t prove you’re a good trader. Screenshots can be fabricated, cherry picked, or completely devoid of context.

Someone can make $10,000 by taking absolutely horrible trades, overleveraging, ignoring risk management, and getting bailed out by luck. That doesn’t make the decisions good it just means the outcome happened to be favorable.

And that’s the biggest problem with judging traders strictly by P&L:

You cannot teach someone how to trade by showing them a P&L.

A P&L tells you what happened. It doesn’t tell you:

* Why the entry was taken
* Why the exit was taken
* What the risk was
* What the invalidation was
* Whether the setup was actually repeatable
* Whether the trader followed a process
* Whether luck played a major role

Give me a trader with consistent execution, defined risk, repeatable setups, and disciplined decision-making over someone posting a massive green screenshot any day.

Profit is the result. The process is the skill.

If your entire argument is “look at my P&L,” you haven’t proven that you’re a skilled trader.

You’ve only proven that you made money that time.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bvrt/psa*

### Post #2647: 2026-09-01 23:29

**📈 A SIMPLE INVESTING STRATEGY FOR BUILDING WEALTH**


You don’t need 30 different stocks to build a diversified portfolio. You need a plan, consistency, and a portfolio that matches your risk tolerance.

Here’s a simple framework:

🏦 CORE — 60%
Build your foundation with broad-market and dividend exposure.

• 40% → Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO)
• 20% → Schwab U.S. Dividend Equity ETF (SCHD)

🚀 GROWTH — 20%
Use a smaller portion for companies with higher growth potential.

• 10% → AST SpaceMobile (ASTS)
• 10% → ServiceNow (NOW)

🌎 MACRO — 10%
Exposure to longer-term themes and macroeconomic trends.

• MP Materials (MP)
• Uranium Energy Corp (UEC)
• Strategy (MSTR)

🛡️ STABILITY — 10%
Companies with established businesses and defensive characteristics.

• Johnson & Johnson (JNJ)
• PepsiCo (PEP)

🔄 THE SCALING PRINCIPLE

The most important part isn’t starting with a huge amount of money.

Start with a structure you can consistently contribute to.

$100 → establish the portfolio
$500 → scale each position proportionally
$1,000 → scale the same strategy further

The goal isn’t to find the next 100x stock.

The goal is to build a system you can stick with as your capital grows.

This is an educational portfolio framework, not individualized financial advice. Higher-growth and concentrated positions can carry substantially more risk.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/trynamakeit/bvrX/a-simple-investing-strategy-fo*
